Generated offscreen submit also uses `owns_viewport_submission()` so only the viewport-terminal child consumes pending graphics-debugger capture, commits shared viewport record/stats, and records capture/runtime-feedback state. Temporal history is now keyed per selected camera: non-owner children render with their own history handle, drain renderer GPU feedback, and update only that camera history slot without mutating shared capture or stats. Linear conversion still samples the framework final color and writes the prepared linear target through the fullscreen conversion pass when the child owns output writes. `RenderCameraTargetWritebackReport` and `RenderCameraTargetGraphImportReport` expose skipped-direct-import, copied, converted, blocked, pending, ready, direct-imported, and camera-stack-suppressed state plus extent/count evidence through `RenderStats.last_camera_target_writeback`, `RenderStats.last_camera_target_graph_import`, `render.camera.target.writeback.*`, and `render.camera.target.graph_import.*`. Prepared WGPU texture handles remain inside renderer resources and are not exposed through framework DTOs.

The runtime framework separates offscreen submit/capture from native surface present. Generated offscreen extract submit now enters `submit_frame_extract/submit/camera_loop.rs`: it resolves `RenderViewExtract.cameras` with `resolve_camera_sequence(...)`, flattens Base cameras followed by their Overlay descriptors into descriptor-only `CameraLoopSubmission` work items, and invokes the existing single-camera renderer body for every child while holding one framework operation lock. The submit loop projects descriptors into selected-camera extracts at the point of invocation, moving the source extract into the terminal child and cloning only for earlier child submissions. Direct `submit_runtime_frame(...)` uses the same descriptor loop through `submit_camera_loop_frame(...)`: it streams one mutable `ViewportRenderFrame`, restores only source fields that affect the next camera context, and selects each camera in place before invoking the selected-frame renderer body. That makes Base/Overlay descriptors real submit work items on both generated extract submit and direct runtime-frame submit, but it still reuses the current per-frame context, capture, record, stats, post-process, history, and lighting ownership model at the viewport-terminal owner. The loop passes shared UI only to the terminal child of the last `PrimarySurface` Base stack, falling back to the last Base stack for texture/headless-only sequences. It also tags stack-terminal and viewport-terminal children separately; today the renderer uses stack-terminal ownership to decide whether the child may mutate a texture output target.

The 2026-06-24 Plan 09 camera-loop test owner split keeps that selected-camera submit path documented as a production owner plus test children. `graphics/runtime/render_framework/submit_frame_extract/submit/camera_loop.rs` now holds only production loop/stream helpers and `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;`; `graphics/runtime/render_framework/submit_frame_extract/submit/camera_loop/tests.rs` owns sequence/extract coverage and shared fixtures; `graphics/runtime/render_framework/submit_frame_extract/submit/camera_loop/tests/frame.rs` owns direct runtime-frame projection coverage and frame-only helpers. Structure guard `runtime_15_render_submit_camera_loop_tests_are_child_owner` locks the split, moved-test/helper boundaries, and docs/status anchors under `render_plan09_camera_loop_test_owner_split_static_passed_cargo_deferred_active_editor_lane` plus `render_plan09_camera_loop_test_helper_owner_split_static_passed_cargo_deferred_active_editor_lane`.

Runtime 07 profiling now exposes the direct runtime-frame selected-camera body with the same render-framework CPU segmentation as generated submit/present: `build_submission_context`, `prepare_runtime_submission`, `render_frame_with_pipeline`, and `collect_runtime_feedback` are separate spans under `submit_runtime_frame`. The spans are profiling-only instrumentation and do not alter the selected-camera loop, viewport-terminal ownership, or render product DTOs.

Each renderer-bound `ViewportRenderFrame` now derives a `ViewportCameraStackAttachmentPolicy` from the selected child descriptor. The policy stores clear intent as a `ViewportSceneClearPlan` rather than relying on WGPU attachment clears, because `LoadOp::Clear` affects the whole texture view. `RenderPassExecutionContext::attachment_ops_for_write(...)` converts graph-declared first `scene-color` and `scene-depth` clear writes into loads, preserving graph store ops and later scene load-store writes. `SceneRegionClearResources` then resolves Base `Skybox` preview color, explicit `Color`, Base `DepthOnly`, the MSAA transparent-color edge case, and Overlay `clear_depth` into a pre-graph color/depth draw clipped to the selected `ViewportRenderRegion`. The physical color/depth backing is the fixed renderer-owned `OffscreenTarget`; `bind_frame_graph_resources(...)` imports live `scene-color` and `scene-depth` rows to those fixed views on every selected-camera child, so load ops preserve the previous Base/Overlay graph result instead of switching to graph-owned transient textures. The same frame carries `ViewportCameraStackOutputPolicy`; `select_final_target_output(...)` owns final-target import selection and reports texture-target graph imports suppressed by non-stack-terminal camera-stack children, while `suppress_output_target_writeback(...)` records the matching writeback suppression. For generated offscreen and direct runtime-frame multi-camera submit, all children resolve and record a per-camera temporal history slot, but only the viewport-terminal child updates shared viewport capture report, previous-state snapshots, virtual-geometry debug snapshot, and stats. `ViewportRenderRegion` also projects the selected descriptor's clamped `viewport_rect` into WGPU viewport and scissor state for graph-raster camera passes, covering split-screen raster isolation for mesh, deferred, sprite, particle, preview-sky, and scene overlay draws. Terminal post-process applies the same region in the coordinate space owned by the target: output-transfer uses local origin-zero coordinates when writing graph-owned `FINAL_COMPOSITED`, while FXAA and the final SMAA resolve use the physical target region and origin when writing imported/full-frame `FINAL_COLOR`. Post-process shaders translate fixed full-frame source/history reads through the selected camera origin. Split-screen PrimarySurface product evidence now passes for independent left/right Base camera clears. Final custom-target pixel/product composite evidence and deeper independent per-camera light, motion-vector, virtual-geometry, and particle state remain later slices.

Native surface present now shares the selected-camera loop. `present_frame_extract_with_ui(...)` derives the viewport-terminal camera target up front, rejects non-primary terminal targets for surface present, requires a bound viewport surface, and then calls `submit_camera_loop(...)` with the present child body. Non-owner Base/Overlay children render through the offscreen pipeline with their own selected-camera history handle and no surface lease, then record only that history slot; only the viewport-terminal child leases the bound surface, calls `renderer.present_frame_with_pipeline(...)`, records the present submission, updates previous-state/history/stats, and consumes graphics-debugger capture. Direct `submit_runtime_frame(...)` now also fans the provided runtime frame through selected-camera child frames and uses the same viewport-terminal owner gate, while keeping final custom-target composite and the remaining independent per-camera state channels as later renderer work. The backend surface path in `render_backend/viewport_surface.rs:55-80` acquires the current WGPU surface texture, blits the rendered offscreen source into it, calls `surface_texture.present()`, and treats outdated/lost/timeout/occluded surfaces as nonfatal or reconfigurable. `viewport_surface.rs:270-330` configures the surface with clamped size, an SRGB-preferred format, `AutoVsync` or FIFO-biased present mode, and fixed frame latency.

The focused surface-target tests document this product boundary. `zircon_runtime/src/graphics/tests/surface_targets.rs` proves offscreen submit/capture survives surface unbind, `Headless { size }` controls captured frame size, headless surface-present requests return explicit unsupported-capability errors, and the surface present implementation does not regress into readback-based present fallback. `zircon_runtime/src/graphics/tests/surface_targets/texture_target.rs` owns the texture-target half: missing texture assets, sampled-only textures, and non-renderable texture formats fail without primary fallback, valid render-target RGBA8 texture metadata controls offscreen capture size plus `RenderStats.last_camera_target_resolution`, linear `rgba8unorm` texture targets report a submitted `Converted` writeback, matching `rgba8unorm_srgb` texture targets report `DirectImported` graph import plus `SkippedDirectImport` writeback, texture surface-present requests return explicit unsupported-capability errors, and Base+Overlay/independent texture-target guards stay together. Surface targets texture-target tests owner split records `render_plan09_surface_targets_texture_target_test_owner_split_static_passed_cargo_deferred_implementation_cadence` and is locked by `runtime_15_surface_targets_texture_target_tests_are_child_owner`, with `graphics/tests/surface_targets.rs` at 441 lines and `graphics/tests/surface_targets/texture_target.rs` at 452 lines. The child also contains `graphics_camera_target_texture_overlay_stack_preserves_base_composite` as the texture-target Base+Overlay direct-import guard. Its 2026-06-19 focused run builds a 64x48 sRGB texture target, submits a Base red-clear camera with an Overlay clear-none stack child, and verifies stack-terminal capture/import/writeback telemetry plus dominant red pixels, proving the Base color survives the Overlay no-clear path for the direct-import texture target. The 2026-06-20 independent-target guard adds a crate-local output-target texture readback helper and `graphics_camera_target_texture_base_stacks_write_independent_texture_targets`, which submits red and green Base stacks to two different sRGB texture targets in one frame, then reads both prepared textures by `ResourceId` and verifies they are not overwritten by the viewport-terminal target. That guard passed scoped formatting, a runtime `core-min` library check, and direct exact execution from `D:\cargo-targets\zircon-runtime-texture-linear-product-0620\debug\deps\zircon_runtime-c339c28ec98a5de7.exe` with 1 test. The current Plan 09 source owner for wider texture-target camera products is now `zircon_runtime/src/graphics/tests/render_product_camera_targets/texture_target.rs`; it holds `texture_target_overlay_camera_draws_layered_mesh_over_base_clear`, `texture_target_overlay_camera_converts_linear_final_product_after_composite`, and `texture_target_stack_preserves_composite_when_primary_surface_renders_later`. The linear conversion guard builds a Base red-clear stack with a layer-filtered green Overlay mesh, requires `TextureWritebackConversion` / `RequiresConversionWriteback` / `Converted` telemetry, and verifies both the captured frame and prepared output target contain the red Base composite plus green Overlay draw. Historical direct exact execution of that test binary passed 1 test before the 2026-06-23 source-owner migration; the migrated owner still needs a fresh focused WGPU rerun. The historical `graphics_primary_surface_split_screen_base_cameras_clear_only_their_viewport_regions` covered two PrimarySurface Base cameras with left/right viewport clears; it first exposed physical scissor use on a local graph-owned terminal target, and after the terminal coordinate split its focused run passed with left/right dominant pixel assertions. On 2026-06-23 the current source guard moved to `render_product_camera_targets/primary_surface.rs::render_product_split_screen_viewports`, and the old split-screen test plus its `surface_targets.rs`-local `primary_base_camera(...)`, region-dominance helpers, and `pixel_at(...)` helper were removed. Scoped rustfmt/static/diff checks passed for the migration, but the focused locked rerun was rejected before compilation because the current `Cargo.lock` would need an update under `--locked`; the migrated split-screen guard still needs a fresh focused WGPU rerun with the rest of the camera-target group after the lock drift is reconciled. Runtime-frame tests prove the resolved output-target marker reaches both generated extract submits and direct runtime-frame submits. Writeback and graph-import unit coverage proves ready, converted, direct-imported, blocked, and camera-stack-suppressed plans map into the neutral telemetry reports before diagnostics consumes them. The surface-target module also guards the surface present implementation against regressing into readback-based present fallback.

## Presentation Gap Classification

| Bevy presentation area | Zircon product state | Completion requirement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Window surface lifecycle | Zircon has viewport records, raw Win32 surface binding, WGPU surface configuration, present blit, and explicit missing-surface errors. It does not yet have Bevy's render-app `ExtractedWindows` / `WindowSurfaces` resource lifecycle or broad platform surface owner model inside the render plan. | Keep native-window ownership in `zircon_app`/platform, but expose enough render-side diagnostics for bound/unbound/resized/present-mode state without mixing platform input work into the renderer. |
| Image and texture render targets | Bevy supports `RenderTarget::Image` and manual `TextureView`; Zircon has a neutral `Texture(handle)` target that resolves valid render-target texture metadata for offscreen submit/capture sizing, carries the resolved texture handle and size on renderer-bound frames, prepares that texture through a dedicated `ResourceStreamer` output-target cache, maps asset `RenderImageUsage::RenderTarget` into WGPU `RENDER_ATTACHMENT` plus copy usages for renderable RGBA8 asset formats, retains sampled binding only when the descriptor also declares `RenderImageUsage::Sampled`, imports prepared `rgba8unorm_srgb` texture targets as the graph final target aliases, skips post-graph writeback for that direct-import path, reads capture results from the prepared target, converts the framework final color into prepared linear `rgba8unorm` targets, and can feed a prepared sampled render target into a later material texture slot. | Broaden output-format policy, support imported external texture views, add target-aware async capture/result APIs, and land multi-camera scheduling before claiming Bevy image/texture-view target parity. |
| No-color / headless target semantics | Bevy `RenderTarget::None { size }` is no-color, useful for prepass-only rendering. Zircon `Headless { size }` renders to an offscreen color target that can be captured. | Record the intentional divergence. Add a true no-color/depth-only target only if depth-prepass or shadow-only camera workflows need it. |
| Screenshot and capture workflow | Bevy has an async screenshot component, per-target preparation, GPU copy-to-buffer, row-padding cleanup, and image callback. Zircon exposes viewport `capture_frame(...)` and RenderDoc capture hooks, but no Bevy-like screenshot request/result pipeline. | Add a screenshot/capture request API that can target primary, headless, and future texture targets, return structured async results, and integrate with dev/CI artifact paths. |
| Present-mode and surface diagnostics | Zircon chooses an advertised present mode internally and records present failures as framework errors; Bevy exposes present-mode fallback decisions in the window render path. | Add surface-format/present-mode/fallback diagnostics to `RenderStats` or `DiagnosticStore` once the platform/window session's surface lifecycle stabilizes. |

## M10R Default 2D And Presentation Base Gate

M10R ties the default 2D and presentation milestones together only at the acceptance boundary. Bevy keeps the same separation: `2d_bevy_render` is the renderer collection for 2D apps, while camera targets and screenshots are target-aware render output contracts. A sprite draw can prove renderer product state only when Core2d extraction, queueing, graph pass execution, resource fallback, and stats are visible; a screenshot or headless capture can prove presentation only when the target and readback path are explicit.

The Zircon side follows that split. [Render Sprite Contracts](../core/framework/render/sprite.md) owns the default Core2d sprite base: `Sprite2dComponent` projects into `RenderSpriteSnapshot`, `SpriteExtract` stays separate from particles, `default_core2d()` exposes sprite graph passes, `RenderSpriteImageMode` covers stretch/tiled/sliced CPU vertex expansion, and sprite stats report ready/fallback counts. This document owns the presentation base: `PrimarySurface` uses the bound viewport surface, `Headless { size }` uses offscreen capture size, `Texture(handle)` resolves valid render-target texture metadata for offscreen submit/capture sizing, and headless or texture surface-present is rejected before it can fall back to the primary surface.

The promotion evidence must include both sides. `render_product_sprite` and the default Core2d pipeline compile test cover the current 2D base; `camera_target` and `surface_targets` cover target routing, offscreen capture size, missing target errors, and swapchain present rather than readback fallback. Mesh2d/SpriteMesh, atlas and nine-slice asset import/editor workflows, Bevy-like binned batching, per-view 2D pipeline specialization, target-aware async screenshot requests, render-to-texture, and manual texture views remain open after M10R.

## Bevy PBR Material And Lighting Evidence

Bevy's PBR baseline is a product family, not just a shader. `dev/bevy/crates/bevy_pbr/src/lib.rs:130-156` defines `PbrPlugin` with prepass, deferred lighting, GPU instance buffer building, and glTF StandardMaterial defaults. The plugin loads the PBR shader library set at `lib.rs:179-198`, registers `StandardMaterial` and `MaterialPlugin::<StandardMaterial>` at `lib.rs:203-216`, adds SSAO, fog, lightmap, light probes, volumetric fog, SSR, transmission, clustered decals, and contact shadows at `lib.rs:217-230`, syncs directional/point/spot/rect/ambient lights at `lib.rs:232-239`, adds atmosphere and GPU clustering at `lib.rs:240-244`, and conditionally adds deferred PBR lighting at `lib.rs:251-252`.

Bevy's `StandardMaterial` carries a broad authored and GPU-facing contract. `dev/bevy/crates/bevy_pbr/src/pbr_material.rs:26-57` starts the material with base color, UV channel, and texture dependency bindings, while later fields cover emissive, metallic/roughness, normal/occlusion, alpha, transmission, clearcoat, anisotropy, and parallax families. The shader-visible flags at `pbr_material.rs:967-1003` distinguish texture slots, double-sided, unlit, normal-map options, fog, parallax, transmission, clearcoat, anisotropy, specular, and alpha modes. `pbr_material.rs:1010-1056` packs the GPU uniform with base color, emissive, roughness, metallic, transmission, thickness, IOR, clearcoat, anisotropy, flags, alpha cutoff, and parallax parameters.

The Bevy material pipeline is asset-driven and render-phase aware. `dev/bevy/crates/bevy_pbr/src/material.rs:74-144` defines `Material` as an `Asset + AsBindGroup` abstraction used with `Mesh3d` and `MeshMaterial3d`; `material.rs:289-342` initializes specialized material pipeline caches, material instances, bind group allocators, draw commands for shadow/transparent/opaque/alpha-mask phases, material mesh specialization, queueing, bind group preparation, shadow specialization, and shadow queueing. `mesh_material.rs:8-41` makes the mesh-to-material handle component explicit, while `material_bind_groups.rs:36-115` shows the bindless/non-bindless allocator and slab resource tracking surface that backs material bind groups.

The shader side confirms why PBR parity must include both forward and deferred lighting. `dev/bevy/crates/bevy_pbr/src/render/pbr.wgsl:65-89` creates `PbrInput` from StandardMaterial bindings, handles alpha discard, writes deferred/prepass output when configured, or applies PBR lighting plus in-shader post-lighting in forward mode. `deferred/deferred_lighting.wgsl:59-86` reconstructs `PbrInput` from the deferred G-buffer, folds in SSAO when available, and applies the same PBR lighting path. `render/pbr_lighting.wgsl:34-122` defines the BRDF and lighting input model for point, spot, and directional lights, including clearcoat and anisotropy variants. Cluster data is its own GPU surface: `cluster/cluster.wgsl:4-24` defines point/spot/probe/decal clusterable object kinds and cluster metadata, and `render/light.rs:1316-1343` writes point/spot lights into `GpuClusteredLight`; `render/light.rs:1519-1624` builds per-view `GpuLights` with ambient, directional, clusters, and rect-light storage.

## Zircon PBR Material And Lighting State

Zircon's current baseline intentionally lands below Bevy's full PBR plugin breadth. The neutral material descriptor in `zircon_runtime/src/core/framework/render/material/standard_material.rs` carries name, dependency set, base color, base-color/normal/metallic-roughness/occlusion/emissive textures, matching per-slot texture transforms, metallic, roughness, emissive, alpha mode, unlit, double-sided, cast/receive shadow policy, material-owned render-order offsets, and fallback policy. `render/material/readiness_report.rs` records validation errors and fallback usage so runtime stats can distinguish usable material rows from fallback-dependent rows.

Concrete GPU preparation projects that descriptor into both runtime material metadata and renderer-owned WGPU material resources. `resource_streamer_ensure_material.rs` loads the material, optional shader contract, and readiness report; resolves the standard base-color, normal, metallic-roughness, occlusion, and emissive texture slots; copies their transform metadata; merges shader readiness; and constructs `MaterialRuntime`. The material report now includes asset-owned shader payload readiness rows from `ShaderAsset::readiness_report()`, so invalid shader entry stages and duplicate or empty shader definitions are visible beside schema, WGSL capture, texture fallback, and missing runtime WGSL diagnostics. `material_runtime.rs` stores the runtime scalar/texture/transform fields, production `alpha_cutoff`, material-owned render-order offsets, optional resolved `RenderQueueValue`, `PipelineKey`, and readiness report; `pipeline_key.rs` keys shader revision, double-sided, alpha blend/mask/cutoff, unlit, and standard texture-slot presence while intentionally excluding cast/receive shadow policy, sort offsets, and transform values. `GpuMaterialUniformResource` keeps authored shader-property uniform payloads separate from a standard-material uniform used by built-in WGPU mesh/deferred/prepass/shadow shaders, so custom property layouts keep their offsets while renderer paths consume stable PBR scalar, UV-transform, shading-model, reactive-mask, and alpha-cutoff channels.

Renderer texture preparation now accepts packed RGBA8 D2 array payloads and complete RGBA8 mip chains through `TextureAsset::upload_readiness(...)`. `GpuTextureResource::from_asset(...)` uploads each mip/layer rectangle into the backing WGPU D2 texture in mip-major, layer-major order, matching the neutral RHI `TextureCopyRegion` direction. The WGPU texture allocation now maps `RenderImageDescriptor.usage` into concrete usage bits, including `RenderTarget` to `RENDER_ATTACHMENT` for renderable RGBA8 formats, copy flags to copy usages, and storage only for formats WGPU can bind as storage; the upload path still adds `COPY_DST` for payload writes. The current material and sprite bind group ABI still expects a D2 sampled texture, so the prepared resource creates a first-layer D2 view for existing PBR/sprite texture slots. `ResourceStreamer::ensure_scene_resources(...)` also prepares a resolved texture camera output target through `OutputTargetTextureResource` after target preflight, giving writeback execution a resident renderable target resource without changing material/sprite binding semantics or forcing sampled binding usage onto render-target-only camera outputs. `ResourceStreamer::execute_output_target_writeback(...)` then copies final offscreen color into that resource when the target descriptor is `rgba8unorm_srgb`, or writes through a renderer-owned conversion pass when the target descriptor is linear `rgba8unorm`, with both paths requiring matching source, plan, and destination extents. This lets render-target-marked RGBA8, six-layer skybox/cubemap-style, and sprite-array payloads reach GPU residency without silently changing shader sampling semantics; array/cube sampling, direct graph render-to-texture, and broader target-format policy still need explicit shader/material and camera scheduling contracts before they can be claimed.

Zircon has real renderer hooks, but they are still narrower than Bevy's PBR path. Deferred geometry is graph-owned through `deferred.gbuffer`, backed material/albedo/normal targets feed `lighting.deferred`, and the current material-channel ABI covers built-in metallic, roughness, occlusion, emissive, unlit, receive-shadow policy, and standard texture transform data without claiming Bevy's full `StandardMaterial` GPU model. Lighting now uses GPUScene light rows plus the light-grid contract for forward/deferred shader iteration. The shadow feature contributes a live `shadow-atlas` graph pass with `shadow.atlas` executor metadata: it writes only the persistent `SHADOW_ATLAS` external resource, records opaque and alpha-mask casters through `ShadowMapRenderer`, and uses Plan 04 shadow-view filtering for directional cascades, point faces, and spot atlas slots when visibility data is available. Forward and deferred receivers now sample through atlas slot resources only; product contracts cover CSM slot generation and multi-spot coexistence, and captured WGPU guards now prove directional plus multi-spot receiver darkening, CSM subtexel camera-shift stability, Forward+/Deferred directional shadow darkening parity, a spot receiver-edge PCF quality delta, and plugin-driven contact-shadow darkening in a Forward+ product frame. Caster expansion to broader scenes, broader contact-shadow scenes, probes, transmission, and advanced material lobes remain outside the accepted baseline.

## PBR Material And Lighting Gap Classification

| Bevy PBR area | Zircon product state | Completion requirement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| StandardMaterial surface | Zircon covers the core base-color, normal, metallic/roughness, occlusion, emissive, alpha, unlit, and double-sided descriptor surface. | Add the missing Bevy StandardMaterial families deliberately: reflectance/specular, transmission/thickness/IOR/attenuation, clearcoat, anisotropy, parallax/depth maps, UV transforms/channels, lightmap interaction, and debug/shader-def controls. |
| Material bind groups | Zircon prepares runtime textures and pipeline keys, but material binding is not Bevy's `AsBindGroup`/bindless allocator model. | Land explicit bind-group layout reflection, slot validation, fallback resource residency, material cache invalidation, and bindless/non-bindless policy before claiming Bevy-like material plugin parity. |
| Phase-specialized material pipeline | Zircon has Core3d phases plus forward/deferred pipeline assets and alpha-derived queues. | Add material-specialized pipeline cache states, per-material shader defs, shadow/deferred/prepass variants, OIT/transmission phases, and structured pipeline-error diagnostics. |
| Physically based lighting | Zircon consumes authored ambient light, GPUScene-backed light rows, the light-grid contract, an LS-M3 `shadow-atlas` graph pass that writes the persistent atlas without the legacy `SHADOW_MAP` receiver, and a plugin-owned contact-shadow WGPU product guard. | Finish broader caster/receiver coverage, broader contact-shadow scenes, rect/area lights, lightmaps, probes/IBL, SSAO/SSR coupling, clearcoat/anisotropy/transmission lighting, and per-view light visibility before marking full PBR lighting complete. |
| Deferred parity | Zircon records a G-buffer geometry pass, backs albedo/normal/material G-buffer resources, preserves the receive-shadow material flag in G-buffer alpha, and runs a fullscreen lighting pass that consumes material channels. | Align the remaining G-buffer contract with stable scalar uniform slots, normal/motion/depth prepasses, deferred lighting pass IDs, SSAO/specular occlusion, and fallback handling for unlit and unsupported material modes. |
| Authoring and assets | Zircon has runtime descriptors and asset-side material files, but this docs slice does not enter `.zmaterial` or material editor implementation. | Sequence `.zmaterial`, material editor projection, shader-contract authoring, and asset hot-reload with the active asset/material lane rather than folding it into render submit docs. |

## M10S Default 3D PBR And Light Gate

M10S uses this document as the default 3D/PBR/light side of the M10.5 gate. The current Zircon state is a foundation, not parity: material descriptors and readiness reports are typed, runtime materials carry a compact `PipelineKey`, ambient light can feed the shared scene uniform, light readiness splits ready/degraded families, the renderer records a G-buffer pass, fullscreen deferred lighting pass, and a limited directional clustered-light compute pass.

The gate remains explicit about what is missing. Bevy's PBR product includes StandardMaterial lobes for transmission, clearcoat, anisotropy, parallax, specular/reflectance, UV channel variants, material bind-group generation, phase specialization, point/spot clusterable lights, rect lights, shadows, contact shadows, probes, lightmaps, SSAO, SSR, fog, volumetrics, and deferred/forward coupling. Zircon must preserve those as open gaps instead of letting an ambient uniform, GPU light-buffer rows, a G-buffer skeleton, the transitional `shadow-atlas`/legacy-receiver bridge, or advanced GI provider close the default 3D milestone.

Promotion evidence must be family-based. Material tests need to distinguish covered descriptor fields from missing Bevy families; light tests need ready/degraded evidence for ambient, directional, point, spot, rect, probe, and baked light families; submit tests need material fallback and light stats; renderer tests need forward/deferred phase and pipeline coverage. `.zmaterial`, material editor projection, shader import resolution, and asset hot-reload remain owned by the active asset/material lane.

The basic forward and deferred mesh shaders share `SceneUniform`. When preview lighting is enabled, `SceneUniform::from_frame(...)` now reads active authored ambient lights from `RenderFrameExtract::lighting.ambient_lights`, accumulates `color * intensity`, and writes that value to `ambient_color`. If no ambient light is authored, the renderer keeps the existing preview fallback ambient value. This closes the first concrete ambient-light consumption step without changing `.zshader` / `.zmaterial` ownership or adding a new material pipeline.

Render submit stats now split light slots by renderer readiness as well as by total count. `RenderLightReadinessReport` in `render::light` owns the rule: `last_ambient_light_ready_count` / `last_ambient_light_degraded_count` report whether authored ambient slots are usable by the current `SceneUniform` path; `last_directional_light_ready_count` is capped to the single directional slot currently consumed by the basic `SceneUniform` path; `last_point_light_ready_count` is capped to the fixed point-light uniform slots consumed by the forward fallback and deferred lighting shaders; extra point lights, spot lights, and rect lights remain degraded until clustered/Forward+, cone, and area-light shader paths land. This mirrors the Bevy distinction between ambient/direct/clusterable/rect light GPU representations without letting the advanced lighting gap hide inside a single total count.

Core pipeline selection is neutral framework data. Cameras select `CorePipelineKind::Core2d` for orthographic projections and `CorePipelineKind::Core3d` for perspective projections. Unset viewport submit uses that extract-owned pipeline kind to choose the built-in Core2d or Forward+ Core3d pipeline, while explicit viewport pipelines and quality-profile overrides remain authoritative and are rejected at compile time if their `core_pipeline` does not match the submitted extract.

Dynamic resolution is also neutral framework data, not a backend upscaler. `ViewportCameraSnapshot` carries `RenderDynamicResolutionSettings`; `RenderViewExtract::effective_view_size()` remains the physical viewport, present, and UI damage size, while `RenderViewExtract::effective_render_size()` applies the clamped render scale for internal scene resources only. `RenderPipelineAsset::compile(...)` uses that render size for transient texture and buffer descriptors such as `scene-color` and `scene-depth`, and keeps external `viewport-output` imported as the unscaled presentation target. Submit status preserves the same split: `FrameSubmissionContext::size()` is the imported/presentation target, `FrameSubmissionContext::render_size()` is the internal graph extent, `RenderStats.last_frame_target_size` / `last_frame_render_size` expose both values, `RenderStats.last_camera_target_resolution` records the camera target kind plus primary/resolved/effective sizes, and `FrameHistoryStatus.target_size` / `render_size` records the dimensions used for temporal validity. Runtime UI follows the presentation-target side of that split. `render_product_ui_submit_keeps_presentation_target_under_dynamic_resolution` submits a scaled primary-surface frame but keeps `RenderStats.last_ui_target_size` at the unscaled viewport target; `render_product_ui_submit_targets_direct_import_texture_under_dynamic_resolution` submits a scaled frame into an eligible `rgba8unorm_srgb` texture target and requires `last_ui_target_size`, `last_frame_target_size`, camera target resolution, direct graph-import telemetry, skipped writeback, and capture source to all point at the resolved texture extent while `last_frame_render_size` stays scaled. Both cases preserve `postprocess-ui-overlay` graph order. Future FSR/TAA/upscale passes therefore have a stable size contract without making current UI, swapchain ownership, or direct texture-output routing depend on scaled render extents; this does not claim a current upscale pass, dynamic quality controller, external texture-view import, or multi-camera texture target scheduler exists.

Legacy `HistoryResolve` is no longer a pipeline feature or explicit opt-in path. Temporal scene-color history is owned by the TAA path: `RenderFeatureQualitySettings::temporal_history`, `FrameHistorySlot::TaaSceneColor`, `TemporalHistoryStore`, and `temporal.taa-resolve`. Profiles that want this chain use `with_temporal_history(true)` plus TAA anti-alias settings; deterministic no-history captures use `with_temporal_history(false)` rather than a legacy history-resolve switch.

`GeometryExtract` carries phase queues derived from `RenderQueueValue` plus the selected pipeline. Production world extraction reads the alpha hint plus `render_queue`, `material_queue`, `order_in_layer`, and `depth_bias` stored on each `MeshRenderer`, then creates phase inputs from the sorted mesh rows. Queue resolution uses the alpha mode as the default Unity segment, accepts authored Unity-range queue values such as 2900 before phase selection, and clamps small legacy offsets to the material offset window. `StandardMaterialDescriptor.render_queue_value` and `MaterialRuntime.render_queue_value` now preserve that typed material queue snapshot beside the raw authored integer fields retained for import/runtime compatibility; blend materials explicitly authored into the opaque/alpha-test queue range report a material readiness validation row before draw construction consumes the value. After `ResourceStreamer::ensure_scene_resources(...)` prepares materials, mesh draw construction rebuilds the consumed phase queue by folding material runtime queue snapshots, raw renderer offsets, material offsets, and depth bias into typed `RenderQueueValue` and `RenderPhaseSortComponents`. The final Plan 09 `u64` sort key is then packed through `packed_sort_key_u64(...)`, ordering camera order, queue segment, a phase-family domain lane, and tie breaker consistently for phase queues and mesh draw commands while preserving the raw mesh-vector fallback when no phase queue exists.

The 2026-06-23 Plan 09 queue override source guard adds `m4_behavior_layers/queue_override.rs::render_product_queue_override_reorders_draws` under status `render_plan09_queue_override_product_source_guard_static_passed`. The guard builds three overlapping meshes: default Geometry red, queue=2900 opaque green, and Transparent blue. It first asserts the extracted phase queue orders Opaque3d red before Transparent3d green and then Transparent3d blue, then samples the WGPU product center region so the final capture must retain green dominance with blue contribution instead of leaving the red Geometry draw on top. This is a test-only source/product guard and does not change production submit code; focused locked Cargo timed out twice after 184 and 304 seconds without a test result or runnable lib-test binary, the second timeout's residual cargo/rustc processes were stopped, and RenderDoc event-order evidence still needs a clean graphics-debugger window.

Pipeline compile validates that declared renderer stages with product phases have matching `RenderPipelineAsset.phase_mapping` entries. The enforced stage-to-phase mapping covers 2D mesh stages, 3D mesh stages, depth prepass, shadow, deferred, postprocess, UI, overlay, and debug; lighting and ambient occlusion remain product-phase-neutral until a dedicated phase exists. Runtime graph execution calls declared graph stages through `execute_graph_stage`; UI, overlay, sprite, preview sky, depth/normal prepass, shadow-map, forward mesh, Deferred G-buffer, Deferred lighting, Deferred transparent mesh execution, SSAO, clustered-light culling, bloom extraction, and final `post.stack` composition are graph-owned. `RenderPassExecutorRegistry` now owns only executor-id registration, explicit plugin/no-op policy, execution lookup, and compiled-pipeline validation; concrete built-in behavior lives in `builtin_postprocess_executors.rs`, `builtin_scene_executors.rs`, and the existing `preview_sky_executor.rs`, so future renderer-specific executor work has a semantic owner instead of extending one registry file. Descriptor-provided plugin executor ids are no longer backfilled with runtime no-op executors: linked plugin descriptors declare graph topology, and explicit `RenderPassExecutorRegistration` rows prove which plugin owns each pass body. Post-process graph node accounting is now a `RenderGraphStageExecution::record_post_process_graph(...)` responsibility: it clones the effective frame graph, runs node availability against graph-bound resources, and writes both the graph and executed node names into the same `RenderGraphExecutionRecord` without letting `render_compiled_scene(...)` reach into the record/resource pair directly.

M8 neural-network support is represented as a compute-backed capability slot, not as an implemented neural renderer. `RenderCapabilityKind::NeuralCompute`, `RenderFeatureCapabilityRequirement::NeuralCompute`, and `RenderBackendCaps.supports_neural_compute` give plugin render features a stable gate for future ML denoisers, upscalers, or inference-backed passes. `BuiltinRenderFeature::NeuralCompute` and its `neural_compute` descriptor are descriptor-only: explicit feature opt-in records the backend capability requirement but adds no graph pass or executor. Plugin neural descriptors remain the owner for executable async-compute passes, and `RenderPipelineCompileOptions` treats `NeuralCompute` as an explicit capability gate so those plugin passes stay out of the graph until capability opt-in. Runtime activation now checks the full chain: descriptor-only neural passes fail `register_pipeline_asset(...)` when `plugin.neural.*` has no explicit `RenderPassExecutorRegistration`, and executor-backed neural passes still fail `set_pipeline_asset(...)` when the active backend capability summary lacks `neural_compute`. The WGPU backend reports the capability as unsupported by default, capability summaries and diagnostics expose that truth, and compiled pipeline validation rejects a descriptor that requires `neural_compute` until a real backend/provider path opts in. Runtime still does not own model weights, tensor lifetimes, or plugin-private neural state.

Post-process effect-stack settings now travel through the same submit path as bloom and color grading. `RenderPostProcessEffectStackSettings` carries neutral tonemap, LUT, blur, motion-blur, depth-of-field, screen-space-reflection, vignette, film-grain, dither, chromatic-aberration, and fog parameters on `PostProcessExtract`; the shared context builder preserves the effective settings and derives enabled-only postprocess product nodes before final composite. The compiled-scene resource registry imports `postprocess.effect-stacked`, `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.depth-pyramid`, `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.specular-occlusion`, and `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.history` for stats/resource validation. `build_post_process_params(...)` now encodes those resolved settings plus camera depth/projection/view-basis parameters into the WGPU post-process uniform block, and `post_process.wgsl` consumes them for baseline tonemap/exposure, authored-or-fallback LUT texture sampling, blur, camera-linearized scene-depth DoF-radius sampling, prepared CoC scratch sampling with center-plus-four-neighbor dilation, prepared bokeh prefilter seed sampling with true center/sample CoC far/near layer weighting, bounded motion-vector gather from graph-reconstructed `postprocess.motion-vector.neighbor-max` with scene-depth foreground-sample attenuation, split SSR depth-pyramid/specular-occlusion/resolve passes, resolved SSR sampling in final composition, vignette, grain, dither, chromatic aberration, and view-depth-weighted fog. Motion blur now uploads `PostProcessParams.effect_motion_blur`, samples the reconstructed graph target, depth-weights foreground sample rejection, and can consume camera/background velocity from `velocity-camera` plus dynamic mesh object velocity from `velocity-object` before `motion-vector-tile-max`, `motion-vector-tile-max-coarse`, and `motion-vector-neighbor-max`; skinned and particle velocity remain absent. The WGPU postprocess bind group treats positive LUT intensity as the request signal, selects an authored 2D or 2D-strip LUT texture through `ResourceStreamer` at binding 10 when prepared, selects a decoded 3D LUT through the dedicated post-process LUT cache at binding 12 when the retained descriptor is `Texture3d`, binds a clamp/linear LUT sampler at binding 13 for the 3D path, falls back to the renderer-owned 64x1 S-curve LUT or 2x2x2 identity cube when the selected authored texture is unavailable, binds graph `scene-depth` at binding 11, feeds binding 1 ambient occlusion into the split SSR specular-occlusion pass with a neutral white fallback, binds graph `gbuffer-normal` at binding 14 for SSR direction, binds graph `gbuffer-material` at binding 16 only when an active pass writes it, binds graph `postprocess.depth-of-field.coc` at binding 17 for final CoC radius consumption, binds graph `postprocess.depth-of-field.bokeh` at binding 18 for final bokeh prefilter seed sampling, binds graph `postprocess.motion-vector.neighbor-max` at binding 19 for final motion-vector sampling, binds `history.previous.screen-space-reflection` at binding 20 for split SSR feedback sampling, binds current `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.history` at binding 21 for final composition, binds split SSR specular occlusion at binding 22, and binds split SSR depth-pyramid ranges at binding 23. The resolve pass deliberately leaves binding 21 on the fallback view while writing the SSR history color attachment so WGPU never sees the same texture as a sampled input and render attachment in that pass. `RenderColorLookupTextureLayout` records whether authored LUT content is `Auto`, `Texture2dStrip { size }`, or `Texture3d { size }`; `ResourceStreamer` keeps each prepared texture's neutral `RenderImageDescriptor` so array and malformed shapes cannot be accidentally bound into the wrong WGPU slot. Text `.cube` LUT files now enter through the built-in `zircon.builtin.texture.cube_lut` importer as linear D3 RGBA8 `TextureAsset` payloads and explicitly reject 1D shaper sections until shaper-aware baking is designed, so submit-time authored LUT handles can drive the same renderer-private 3D path without `app`, `editor`, or framework code depending on WGPU. `PostProcessParams.effect_flags.y` records the actual shader binding mode selected for the frame, and the 3D branch samples normalized texel-center coordinates with `textureSampleLevel(...)` so endpoint colors remain stable while intermediate values interpolate between LUT cells. `PostProcessParams.effect_projection` carries perspective focal scales and orthographic half extents so the SSR resolve path can reconstruct a view-space origin from depth and project ray candidates back to screen pixels before depth/thickness acceptance, while `effect_view_x/y/z` carries the camera basis used to transform the world-space normal buffer into the view space used by that ray march. `RenderStats.last_post_process_effect_stack_report` records `effect-stack.lut.texture` only when LUT intensity is authored without a texture handle, records `effect-stack.lut.texture-layout` for invalid explicit layout sizes, treats a supplied authored LUT as renderer-bound rather than approximation-backed, clears `effect-stack.ssr.normal` when the graph declares `gbuffer-normal`, clears SSR temporal history when the graph declares `history.previous.screen-space-reflection`, clears `effect-stack.motion-blur.motion-vector` when the graph declares `postprocess.motion-vector.neighbor-max`, and keeps `effect-stack.motion-blur.velocity-prepass` until `temporal.velocity-camera`, `temporal.velocity-object`, `post.motion-vector-tile-max`, `post.motion-vector-tile-max-coarse`, and `post.motion-vector-neighbor-max` execute; material roughness stays a renderer-bound G-buffer input with black-fallback roughness 1.0 when no writer exists. `RenderStats.last_mesh_previous_velocity_transform_draw_count`, `last_mesh_missing_velocity_transform_draw_count`, and `last_mesh_skinned_gpu_velocity_draw_count` record draw-level eligibility for the object producer; the retired CPU object-history counters are no longer part of this submit contract. `RenderStats.last_post_process_lut_{request,ready,fallback}_count` plus `last_post_process_lut_2d_strip_ready_count`, `last_post_process_lut_3d_request_count`, `last_post_process_lut_unsupported_shape_count`, and `render.post_process.lut.*` diagnostics expose whether the enabled LUT family requested a texture, prepared it, used fallback, matched the 2D-strip contract, used the renderer-private 3D path, or supplied an unsupported shape. Advanced LUT authoring policies, skinned/particle velocity output, a full dynamic multi-level tile-max/neighbor-max motion-blur pyramid, half-resolution bokeh simulation/combines, a full hierarchical SSR depth/reflection pyramid, and deeper dedicated split passes keep explicit regression labels instead of being hidden behind one enabled product node.

The 2026-06-05 SSR reflection-pyramid follow-up extends that submit contract with `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.reflection-pyramid`: the compiled-scene registry imports the concrete half-resolution `Rgba16Float` target, `screen-space-reflection-reflection-pyramid` writes a 2x2 scene-color average before specular occlusion and resolve, and binding 24 supplies that first-level color pyramid to SSR resolve. The remaining SSR pyramid gap is now the full multi-mip hierarchy rather than the first graph-visible color/depth handoff.

The 2026-06-05 SSR coarse-pyramid follow-up adds the fixed second level. `screen-space-reflection-depth-pyramid-coarse` / `post.screen-space-reflection-depth-pyramid-coarse` reads `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.depth-pyramid` and writes quarter-resolution `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.depth-pyramid.coarse`; `screen-space-reflection-reflection-pyramid-coarse` / `post.screen-space-reflection-reflection-pyramid-coarse` reads `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.reflection-pyramid` and writes quarter-resolution `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.reflection-pyramid.coarse`. Both use concrete `Rgba16Float` offscreen targets imported by the compiled-scene registry. Binding 25 exposes coarse depth ranges, binding 26 exposes coarse reflection color, and SSR resolve requires first-level plus coarse depth/reflection resources before writing the current SSR history target. This is a fixed two-level hierarchy, not the final dynamic multi-mip SSR pyramid.

For motion blur, this submit contract now distinguishes raw graph target readiness, half-resolution tile reconstruction readiness, quarter-resolution coarse-tile reconstruction readiness, neighbor reconstruction readiness, camera/background velocity readiness, dynamic mesh object velocity readiness, CPU-morphed skinned previous-shape gaps, and particle velocity gaps. The graph declares and imports raw `scene-velocity`, half-resolution `postprocess.motion-vector.tile-max`, quarter-resolution `postprocess.motion-vector.tile-max.coarse`, and reconstructed `postprocess.motion-vector.neighbor-max`; `temporal.velocity-camera` writes the raw target with compatible previous-camera history, `temporal.velocity-object` writes non-transparent dynamic mesh object velocity for draws that have a previous object transform, `particle.velocity` can write matched particle billboard velocity into the same raw target, `post.motion-vector-tile-max` writes the tile target, `post.motion-vector-tile-max-coarse` writes the coarse tile target, `post.motion-vector-neighbor-max` writes the full-resolution reconstructed target, and submit stats clear the velocity-prepass gap only when the camera/object producer and reconstruction passes execute. The compatibility gate includes projection mode, target, viewport, dynamic-resolution, FOV/orthographic-size/clip-plane jump checks, large translation, and large rotation checks. `RenderStats.last_particle_velocity_missing_sprite_count` records current particle sprites that rendered through `particle.transparent` while reconstructed velocity was requested and no matched `ParticleExtract.previous_sprites` row exists; sprites with matched previous state are excluded from the missing count and are eligible for the V1 `particle.velocity` writer. Richer temporal policy, a full dynamic multi-level tile-max/neighbor-max pyramid, changing CPU-morphed previous-shape velocity, stable multi-sprite particle identity, previous billboard-basis ownership, and particle velocity readback/RenderDoc evidence remain explicit gaps.

The compatibility gate now has a lower invalid-camera floor as well: FOV and clip ranges must be finite and valid, and the current/previous projection matrices plus current inverse matrix must be finite before `temporal.velocity-camera` can write velocity. Failure disables the pass for that frame and leaves the clear-stored `scene-velocity` target neutral.

The submit path resolves neutral post-process volume DTOs before the product graph is built. `PostProcessExtract.volumes` carries global/box/sphere shape snapshots with priority, weight, volume mask, and component override vectors. `PostProcessExtract::resolved_settings_for_camera(...)` evaluates those DTOs against the submitted camera position and render layers through `VolumeEvaluator`, then feature gates decide whether bloom and color grading remain active. The history validation key and renderer frame receive only those resolved settings; raw planned volume DTOs are cleared before renderer execution so concrete postprocess code cannot bypass the graph contract.

Graph attachment operations are now part of this submit boundary.
`RenderPipelineAsset` emits clear/load/store metadata by resource write order, and feature descriptors may explicitly override write ops for target initialization passes such as Deferred preview sky clearing imported `final-color`.
`RenderPassExecutionContext::attachment_ops_for_write(...)` exposes that decision to executors, and graph-owned executors translate the neutral contract through `scene_renderer::attachment_ops`.
`RenderPassExecutionContext` stays the graph metadata and resource-access owner. It now carries an optional `RgResourceResolver` from `render_pass_execution_context/resource_resolver.rs` during staged execution, giving executors a handle-based view of compiled graph declarations and live lifetimes without exposing WGPU objects. Product postprocess resource checks use pass-scoped read declarations from that resolver before requiring texture views, and `RenderGraphExecutionResources` validates declaration texture/buffer kind before falling through to its current name-backed WGPU maps. The same resolver is now propagated into `RenderPassGpuExecutionContext`, where depth-prepass writes, deferred G-buffer writes/reads, deferred lighting G-buffer/light-grid/background/scene-color lookups, mesh-stage color/depth attachments, mesh light-grid reads, TAA reactive-mask mesh attachments, sprite/preview-sky/UI/overlay surface bridge lookups, particle transparent/velocity bridge lookups, SSR-specific resolve/reflection-pyramid/coarse-pyramid/specular-occlusion bridge lookups, and root postprocess stack/effect/compute/terminal bridge lookups resolve through `gpu/resource_lookup.rs` helpers before recording WGPU commands. The mesh light-grid path is optional at the resolver layer so Forward mesh passes require declared buffers while Deferred transparent mesh can keep its no-light-grid pass contract; shared post-process texture inputs use the same optional helper shape to preserve fallback textures for passes that do not declare a slot while still rejecting declared kind mismatches. The renderer-heavy GPU payload and shared scene record scaffolding live in `render_pass_execution_context/gpu.rs`; deferred G-buffer and lighting bridge methods live in `render_pass_execution_context/gpu/deferred.rs`; sprite, screen-space UI, preview-sky, and overlay bridge methods live in `render_pass_execution_context/gpu/surface.rs`; particle bridge methods live in `render_pass_execution_context/gpu/particle.rs`; postprocess stack orchestration stays in `render_pass_execution_context/gpu/post_process.rs`; effect-chain, compute-resource, temporal, terminal, and SSR-specific bridge methods live in `post_process/effects.rs`, `computed_resources.rs`, `temporal.rs`, `terminal.rs`, and `screen_space_reflection.rs`.
`RenderGraphExecutionResources::materialize_transient_resources(...)` backs live dense transient graph textures/buffers with concrete WGPU resources before pass execution, preserves imported frame targets, and deliberately skips sparse-reserved textures so virtual resources cannot be mistaken for dense allocations.
The 2026-06-24 RenderGraph materialization test owner split keeps `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/graph_execution/materialization.rs` as the production WGPU transient materialization owner and moves storage-usage, dense/sparse materialization, aliasing, terminal AA override, mip-view, and SSR coarse-pyramid alias tests into `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/graph_execution/materialization/tests.rs`. Guard `runtime_15_render_graph_materialization_tests_are_child_owner_split` locks that parent/test-child shape under `render_plan01_materialization_tests_owner_split_static_passed_cargo_deferred_active_compile_lane`; the slice has scoped rustfmt/static/line-count/docs-anchor/whitespace/diff-check evidence only, with Cargo/WGPU/RenderDoc deferred because active compile lanes were present.
After target import, optional history import, and transient materialization, `RenderGraphExecutionResources::resource_report()` summarizes the frame registry as neutral counts for texture views, external texture views, renderer-owned transient textures, buffers, and total bound resources. `RenderGraphExecutionRecord` carries that report to `RenderStats.last_graph_execution_resource_report`, and runtime diagnostics mirror it under `render.graph.execution.*`. This is M5 resource-ownership evidence for the graph registry; it still does not expose WGPU handles, allocator residency, pass timings, or sparse page-table state outside the renderer.
`RenderGraphExecutionCoverageReport` compares compiled live graph pass names with the renderer's executed pass record during `update_base_stats(...)`. It records planned-live pass count, executed pass count, matched planned pass count, missing planned pass count, unexpected executed pass count, and duplicate executed pass count in `RenderStats.last_graph_execution_coverage_report`; runtime diagnostics mirror those counts under `render.graph.execution.coverage.*`. `render_framework_stats_report_graph_execution_coverage` submits the default Forward+ frame through `WgpuRenderFramework` and asserts the report matches the live compiled-pass count with zero missing, unexpected, or duplicate executed passes. This is graph cutover coverage evidence for planned-vs-executed pass parity, not a public pass-name dump or execution trace.
`RenderGraphStageExecutionReport` summarizes the renderer-owned stage metadata attached to the executed graph pass sequence. It records staged pass count, unstaged pass count, unique stage count, stage transition count, and backward stage-order violation count, then `update_base_stats(...)` stores it in `RenderStats.last_graph_stage_execution_report`. Runtime diagnostics mirror those counts under `render.graph.execution.stage.*`. `render_framework_stats_report_graph_stage_execution` derives the same neutral stage report from the compiled default Forward+ live pass order and verifies the submitted `WgpuRenderFramework` frame reports the matching aggregate with no stage-order violations. This is pass-order/stage-coverage evidence only; exact stage enums and per-pass WGPU work remain inside the renderer execution record.
`render_framework_submits_advanced_postprocess_graph_passes` is the submitted-frame evidence for the active DoF, motion-vector, SSR, and final-stack chain. It submits the same authored postprocess frame twice, observes `MissingPreviousCamera` on the first motion-vector camera frame and `Ready` on the second, then asserts the expected pass names and executor ids were recorded for motion-vector clear/camera/tile/coarse/neighbor, DoF prepare, split SSR depth/reflection/specular/resolve, and `post.stack`. The test also requires zero graph coverage gaps, active effect-stack families for DoF/motion blur/SSR, final-composite graph execution, and no `effect-stack.motion-blur.velocity-prepass` missing-resource label once the second-frame producer chain is ready.
`sky.preview-scene-color` and `sky.preview-final-color` execute in the `DepthPrepass` stage before geometry, clear the background target plus `scene-depth`, and then let depth/opaque passes load the initialized attachments.
Depth/normal prepass consumes both the `scene-depth` and `gbuffer-normal` write ops before calling `NormalPrepassPipeline::record_with_attachment_ops(...)`.
`shadow.atlas` now writes only the imported persistent `shadow-atlas` external resource. In compiled-scene execution it uses `ShadowMapRenderer` to upload per-slot light-space scene uniforms, sets atlas viewport/scissor rectangles, and records opaque plus alpha-mask casters through the shadow pipelines while per-slot Plan 04 shadow-view filtering narrows directional cascades, point faces, and spot slots when matching visibility exists.
`lighting.deferred` and the forward mesh passes declare `shadow-atlas` reads for receiver ordering; the old single `shadow-map` graph resource and receiver bindings have been removed.
`render_framework_stats_report_shadow_atlas_graph_execution` proves a submitted WGPU RenderFramework frame reports the executed `shadow-atlas` pass, `shadow.atlas` executor id, graph debug marker, and `last_shadow_graph_executed_pass_count`. `render_product_many_point_lights` now covers the 64 point-light source/graph contract: all point lights survive packing, light-grid zbin/tile masks use the second 32-bit word, and Forward+/Deferred graphs consume the same `light-grid-build` buffers. `render_product_csm_directional` and `render_product_multi_spot_shadows` cover CSM slot generation and multi-spot atlas coexistence at the product-contract source layer; captured many-light parity, directional/multi-spot atlas receiver darkening, CSM subtexel camera-shift stability, Forward+/Deferred directional shadow darkening parity, spot PCF quality edge differences, and contact-shadow plugin darkening are now covered by WGPU product guards. Caster expansion, broader receiver/contact-shadow coverage, and RenderDoc evidence remain explicit follow-up work.
`contact_shadow_wgpu_product_capture_darkens_screen_space_contact_region` is the plugin-side product guard for the contact-shadow path. It submits matching Forward+ receiver/blocker frames with and without `rendering.contact_shadow`, requires the `contact-shadow` pass and `lighting.contact-shadow` executor dispatch, checks graph coverage gaps stay zero, and compares final captures for measurable contact-region darkening. The plugin executor resolves pass-declared texture views through `RenderPassGpuExecutionContext::require_texture_view(...)`, keeping WGPU resource access behind the renderer-owned resolver boundary.
The 2026-06-05 alpha-mask shadow cutoff follow-up passed scoped source-level validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check`, shadow shader/renderer/source-contract scans, conflict-marker scan, trailing-whitespace scan, and path-scoped `git diff --check` passed, with only expected LF/CRLF notices from Git. Clean Cargo validation is not claimed: the focused `cargo test -p zircon_runtime --lib shadow_params --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-render-main-chain --message-format short --color never -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture` run timed out after 604.3s without useful compiler/test output, and the remaining compiler lane afterward belonged to unrelated work under `D:\cargo-targets\zircon-drag-drop-stale-event-0605`.
`ao.ssao-evaluate`, `lighting.clustered-cull`, and `post.bloom-extract` require post-process stack context and write graph-bound `ambient-occlusion`, `light-list`, and `bloom-texture` resources at their declared stages.
`post.stack` consumes those resources plus graph-bound `scene-depth`, scene/final/GI targets, and the clustered light-list buffer for final post-process recording only, replacing the former fixed `execute_post_process_stack(...)` wrapper without hiding preparation work inside one pass.
`ui.screen-space` passes the `viewport-output` write ops into `ScreenSpaceUiRenderer::record(...)` before it opens the WGPU render pass.
The 2026-06-24 Screen-space UI render test owner split keeps that production path in `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/ui/render.rs` while moving private planning tests to `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/ui/render/tests.rs`. Guard `runtime_15_screen_space_ui_render_tests_are_child_owner_split` locks the parent/test child shape and docs/status anchors under `render_plan14_screen_space_ui_render_test_owner_split_static_passed_cargo_deferred_active_compile_lane`; the slice has scoped rustfmt/static/line-count/docs-anchor/whitespace/diff-check evidence only, with Cargo/WGPU/RenderDoc deferred because active compile lanes were present.
The 2026-06-24 Screen-space UI SDF atlas test owner split keeps atlas slot planning, glyph-key collection, cache retention/eviction, and glyph run indexing in `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/ui/sdf_atlas.rs` while moving private atlas plan/cache tests to `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/ui/sdf_atlas/tests.rs`. Guard `runtime_15_screen_space_ui_sdf_atlas_tests_are_child_owner_split` locks the parent/test child shape and docs/status anchors under `render_plan14_sdf_atlas_test_owner_split_static_passed_cargo_deferred_active_compile_lane`; the slice has scoped rustfmt/static/line-count/docs-anchor/whitespace/diff-check evidence only, with Cargo/WGPU/RenderDoc deferred because active compile lanes were present.
The 2026-06-24 Screen-space UI SDF render test owner split keeps the SDF WGPU pipeline, atlas resources, glyph resolution, vertex generation, and draw recording in `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/ui/sdf_render.rs` while moving private draw-plan and prepare-report tests to `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/ui/sdf_render/tests.rs`. Guard `runtime_15_screen_space_ui_sdf_render_tests_are_child_owner_split` locks the parent/test child shape and docs/status anchors under `render_plan14_sdf_render_test_owner_split_static_passed_cargo_deferred_active_compile_lane`; the slice has scoped rustfmt/static/line-count/docs-anchor/whitespace/diff-check evidence only, with Cargo/WGPU/RenderDoc deferred because active compile lanes were present.
`overlay.gizmo` requires the graph-bound `viewport-output` and `scene-depth` resources plus prepared overlay buffers before calling `ViewportOverlayRenderer::record_overlays(...)`, so compiled-scene submission no longer draws overlays through a private post-graph call.
Sprite executors now pass separate `scene-color` and `scene-depth` ops into `SpriteRenderer::record(...)`, making the Core2d opaque sprite pass the depth producer and preserving the old Core2d placement by calling graph stages from the compiled-scene main pass order. The same path consumes `RenderSpriteImageMode`: stretch sprites remain one quad, scale sprites use Bevy-style Fit/Fill aspect handling in a single quad or source crop, tiled sprites repeat the source rect along selected axes, and sliced sprites expand the nine-slice border/center/side policy into bounded CPU quads before adjacent texture batching.
The 2026-06-24 Sprite build vertices test owner split keeps phase extraction, selected-camera layer filtering, image-mode CPU slicing, nine-slice/tile helpers, and vertex expansion in `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/sprite/build_sprite_vertices.rs` while moving private phase/filter/scale/tile/slice tests to `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/sprite/build_sprite_vertices/tests.rs`. Guard `runtime_15_sprite_build_vertices_tests_are_child_owner_split` locks the parent/test child shape and docs/status anchors under `render_plan14_sprite_build_vertices_test_owner_split_static_passed_cargo_deferred_active_compile_lane`; the slice has scoped rustfmt/static/line-count/docs-anchor/whitespace/diff-check evidence only, with Cargo/WGPU/RenderDoc deferred because active compile lanes were present.
Forward mesh executors pass `scene-color` ops into `BaseScenePass`, require mesh draw/pipeline context plus `scene-depth`, and split prepared draws into opaque, alpha-mask, and transparent buckets.
Deferred executors pass `gbuffer-albedo`, `gbuffer-material`, and `scene-color` attachment ops into the concrete G-buffer and lighting passes, and `import_frame_targets(...)` binds `gbuffer-albedo`, `gbuffer-material`, and `gbuffer-normal` from the offscreen target set.
The fixed legacy `render_scene(...)` path remains explicit `Load + Store`; new graph-owned renderers should consume pass metadata rather than copy pass-name-specific load/store rules.

M8 compute/storage writes are now separated from attachment writes at the same SRP boundary. `RenderFeatureResourceWriteMode` lets descriptors declare storage outputs that compile to `write_storage_texture(...)` or `write_storage_external(...)`, keeping dependency, culling, queue-lane, and debug-marker evidence without assigning attachment load/store ops. `ao.ssao-evaluate` reads graph `scene-depth` and `gbuffer-normal`, writes `ambient-occlusion` as storage external output, and still declares `QueueLane::AsyncCompute`; when async compute is unavailable, pipeline compile falls it back to the graphics execution queue while preserving the declared async pass count and the no-attachment-ops resource metadata. Descriptor validation rejects storage write mode on read resources and rejects attachment ops on storage writes.

M8 compute workload planning now lives at the descriptor and RenderGraph compile boundary before any renderer-owned GPU object exists. `RenderGraphComputeWorkload` records only a pipeline label, workgroup size, and dispatch extent (`Viewport`, `ClusterGrid`, `HzbFurthest`, `IndirectArgs`, or fixed groups). `RenderFeaturePassDescriptor::with_compute_workload(...)` lets SRP features attach that plan to a pass; `RenderPipelineAsset::compile(...)` validates that it is declared only on `AsyncCompute` passes with a non-empty pipeline label and non-zero workgroup size, then preserves it on the compiled graph pass. The built-in SSAO and clustered-lighting descriptors declare viewport and cluster-grid workload plans through shared descriptor constants; HZB build declares the HZB mip-chain extent; HZB occlusion declares the indirect-args extent. Plugin descriptor fixtures follow the same contract: neural compute and SSAO use viewport workloads, while hybrid-GI trace scheduling and virtual-geometry cluster culling use fixed planned dispatch groups. Compile coverage asserts the compiled graph still carries those plugin workload labels, workgroup sizes, and extents, so plugin async-compute descriptors cannot silently regress to unplanned passes. This is planned metadata for pipeline activation, review, and future plan-vs-execution diagnostics; it is not a WGPU pipeline, bind group, texture, or proof that a dispatch already ran.

The same M8 path now records concrete compute dispatch evidence after the graph executor resolves GPU resources. `RenderGraphComputeDispatchRecord` stores the graph pass name, executor id, renderer-private pipeline label, workgroup size, `[x, y, z]` dispatch group count, and storage-write resource names; the record intentionally does not expose WGPU device, pipeline, bind group, or texture handles. `record_ssao_to_resources(...)` pushes a `zircon-ssao-pipeline` dispatch only when SSAO is runtime-enabled, and `record_clustered_lighting_to_resources(...)` pushes a `zircon-cluster-pipeline` dispatch only when clustered lighting is runtime-enabled. Disabled fallbacks may still clear/write their output buffers, but they do not count as compute dispatches. `execute_graph_stage(...)` drains those GPU-context records into `RenderGraphExecutionRecord`, builds a dispatch audit context from the viewport size, derived cluster grid, HZB extent, and indirect args metadata, then audits them against the compiled pass `RenderGraphComputeWorkload`. For HZB occlusion, the context uses the phase-local dispatch group count reported by `HzbOcclusionCullReport`, so three small opaque/alpha-mask/velocity buffers audit as three dispatch groups instead of one total-args group. The audit compares pipeline label, workgroup size, and planned-versus-actual dispatch groups for `Viewport`, `ClusterGrid`, `HzbFurthest`, `IndirectArgs`, and `Fixed` extents before publishing dispatch and audit counts through `RenderStats.last_graph_compute_*` plus the `render.graph.compute_dispatch_count`, `render.graph.compute_dispatch_group_count`, `render.graph.compute_storage_write_resource_count`, `render.graph.compute_planned_workload_count`, `render.graph.compute_matched_workload_count`, `render.graph.compute_missing_dispatch_count`, `render.graph.compute_workload_mismatch_count`, and `render.graph.compute_unexpected_dispatch_count` diagnostics rows. Async fallback tests can therefore prove a real compute pass body launched and matched the SRP plan without leaking backend objects across the framework boundary.

Renderer-side queue preparation is also surfaced at submit scope. `SceneRendererCompiledSceneOutputs` carries mesh queue stats from `prepare_mesh_queue(...)` and sprite queue stats from `prepare_sprite_queue_stats(...)` back to the render framework. `RenderStats.last_mesh_*` records draw/phase counts, early-z eligibility, shadow-caster eligibility, alpha-mask shadow caster scale, prepared versus dynamic geometry, indirect draws, static/dynamic/GPU-instancing candidate groups, skinned GPU velocity coverage, and CPU-morphed previous-shape velocity gaps. `RenderStats.last_sprite_*` records draw batch count, batched sprite count, generated image-slice count, expanded image-slice count, generated vertex count, and per-phase sprite batch counts. `RenderStats.last_particle_velocity_missing_sprite_count` records current particle sprites that still lack matched previous state when motion blur or temporal SSR request reconstructed velocity; matched rows can feed the V1 `particle.velocity` writer. These counters prove what the graph-owned renderer prepared without exposing WGPU buffers or forcing the future mesh allocator, final particle previous-state ownership policy, and Bevy-like sprite binning work to land first.

Submit safety is guarded by viewport generations. Context building captures the viewport record generation while resolving size, effective pipeline, quality profile, and history state. Before runtime prepare mutates viewport runtime state, and again before recording the rendered frame back into the viewport, submit revalidates that the viewport still exists and that its generation matches. Missing viewports return `RenderFrameworkError::UnknownViewport`; changed viewports return `RenderFrameworkError::ViewportChanged` instead of relying on checked-then-`expect` panics.

Frame history reuse now includes an extract validation key, a selected-camera storage key, and an explicit submit status. The shared context builder records world id, the selected `CameraRenderDescriptor`, mesh identity/transform/model/material/tint/mobility/layer mask, lighting extract, animation pose extract, post-process settings, particle extract, and the compiled effective feature names. `ViewportCameraHistoryKey` first chooses the viewport-record history slot from the selected descriptor's entity, render order, Base/Overlay type, target order key, and viewport rect. The descriptor validation key then checks the payload camera motion plus descriptor-owned target, viewport, render order, clear policy, culling mask, and volume mask, so the Plan 09 hard cutover does not lose history invalidation for fields that moved out of `ViewportCameraSnapshot`. It also carries both target size and render size so dynamic resolution cannot reuse temporal history just because the swapchain or imported viewport target stayed the same. `resolve_history_handle(...)` reuses the previous `FrameHistoryHandle` only when the selected camera slot exists and target size, render size, pipeline, history bindings, and this validation key all match. Camera motion, descriptor target/layer/viewport changes, mesh motion, material/tint/layer changes, light changes, pose changes, bloom/color-grading/preview changes, particle changes, world changes, feature toggles, and dynamic render-scale changes therefore allocate a new history handle before renderer history textures are reused. `ViewportFrameHistory` also stores that camera slot's `VisibilityStaticIndex`, so split-screen Base cameras and Overlay children no longer share one viewport-wide static visibility index.

The compatibility result is carried as `FrameHistoryStatus` in `RenderStats.last_frame_history_status`. The status reports the current handle, previous handle, whether the previous frame was actually usable, target size, render size, and the invalidation reason (`no_previous_frame`, `viewport_resized`, `render_size_changed`, `pipeline_changed`, `history_binding_changed`, or `frame_inputs_changed`). The renderer submit path receives this result as `previous_history_available`, so `prepare_history_textures(...)` no longer treats an existing same-size texture as sufficient proof that temporal history is valid. Renderer history copy also preserves slot semantics for non-TAA history families. TAA scene-color history is keyed as `FrameHistorySlot::TaaSceneColor` and is managed by `TemporalHistoryStore` plus `temporal.taa-resolve`, so bloom/color grading and later overlay/UI composition do not feed back into TAA. Since `render_plan06_temporal_history_texture_lifetime_owner_suppression_coremin_passed`, `TemporalHistoryTexture` keeps the paired underlying `wgpu::Texture` as `_texture`; the field is an explicit lifetime owner for `previous_view()` / `current_view()` rather than a dead public accessor.

`RenderHistoryCopyReport` records the renderer-private history copy step as neutral counts and booleans. It reports whether a history target existed, whether the history-copy debug marker was emitted, how many copy slots were requested, how many were actually copied, the copy target size, and per-slot copy evidence for scene color, global illumination, ambient occlusion, screen-space reflection history, and exposure history. `RenderGraphExecutionRecord` carries that report to `RenderStats.last_frame_history_copy_report`, and runtime diagnostics mirror it under `render.history.copy.*`, including `render.history.copy.exposure_copied`. This is copy and marker evidence only; WGPU textures, texture views, exposure buffers, and command encoder internals remain inside the renderer.

The graph-facing TAA resource names are split by temporal role. `taa.history.previous.scene-color` is the compatible previous TAA scene-color texture imported into the graph, `taa.history.current.scene-color` is the current TAA history target, and `taa.output.scene-color` is the TAA resolve output consumed by final composition when TAA is live. The `temporal.taa-resolve` descriptor reads scene color, depth, scene velocity, previous TAA history, and the reactive mask, then writes `taa.output.scene-color` plus `taa.history.current.scene-color`; the retired `postprocess.history-resolved`, `history.previous.scene-color`, and `history.current.scene-color` scene-color names are intentionally not compiled graph resources.

The effect-stack resource name follows the same explicit-output rule. `postprocess.effect-stacked` is produced only by the authored or volume-resolved `EffectStack` product node and becomes the sole final-composite input when any non-default effect-stack family is active. Default submissions keep the old single final-composite path and do not add a skipped placeholder node.

Temporal SSR follows that graph-visible resource rule instead of reading hidden renderer state. When SSR is active and compatible previous history is available, the `ScreenSpaceReflectionResolve` node declares `history.previous.screen-space-reflection` plus `postprocess.motion-vector.neighbor-max`; the resolve shader reprojects the dedicated previous SSR history texture with the reconstructed motion-vector target and rejects the path through the same history-valid flag used by history resolve.
