The current resolve pass writes `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.history`, final `post.stack` samples that resolved target, and renderer history copy stores the target for the next compatible frame. Submit stats now feed that graph state into `RenderPostProcessEffectStackReport`: missing previous SSR history is reported as `effect-stack.ssr.temporal-history`, while a declared temporal SSR history path with missing reconstructed motion vectors or missing velocity passes reports `effect-stack.ssr.temporal-motion-vector`, `effect-stack.ssr.temporal-velocity-prepass`, and the camera/tile/neighbor stage suffixes. The current SSR split also has graph-visible specular-occlusion, first-level half-resolution depth-pyramid, and first-level half-resolution reflection-pyramid products; full multi-mip depth/reflection pyramid work remains future renderer work.

The 2026-06-05 SSR reflection-pyramid follow-up adds the matching first-level half-resolution color product, `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.reflection-pyramid`, so temporal SSR now sees graph-visible specular occlusion plus first-level depth and reflection pyramid inputs before the remaining multi-mip pyramid work.

The 2026-06-05 SSR coarse-pyramid follow-up adds `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.depth-pyramid.coarse` and `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.reflection-pyramid.coarse` as quarter-resolution graph-visible products. Final submit state now has split specular occlusion plus fixed two-level depth and reflection pyramid inputs before the dynamic multi-mip pyramid work.

Graphics debugger capture is a submit-scoped request, not a persistent rendering mode. The only live triggers are `RenderFramework::request_graphics_debugger_capture(viewport)` and `ZR_RENDERDOC_CAPTURE_NEXT=1`; editor UI and dynamic API commands do not currently expose a separate RenderDoc button. The trait method stores a pending viewport in `WgpuRenderFramework`; non-matching viewport submits leave it pending. The environment variable arms the first viewport created by the framework so desktop debug launches can capture the first rendered frame without editor code calling the trait method. On the matching submit, capture begins before runtime prepare/render command recording and finishes after the frame is produced. The blocking wgpu stop/poll step runs after the framework state mutex is released, while an operation lock remains held so no second frame or viewport/pipeline mutation can enter the active capture window. Destroying a viewport with pending or queued debugger capture clears that debugger state and records a destroyed-viewport error instead of leaving `capture_pending` true forever. The status query reports wgpu capture-hook availability, the selected wgpu backend as `wgpu(dx12)` / `wgpu(vulkan)` / equivalent, pending/active flags, the last captured frame generation, and any submit or stop error. `available` means the backend exposes the wgpu debugger capture hook; it does not prove RenderDoc is attached. If the matching submit fails during preflight before capture starts, the pending request is consumed and `last_error` records the preflight error. If a submit fails while capture is active, cleanup still stops the capture and clears active/pending state before returning the original error.

RenderDoc-readable markers are centralized in `zircon_runtime/src/graphics/debug_markers.rs`. The compiled-scene command encoder emits markers for `FrameExtract`, `Clear`, `Prepass`, `MainScene`, `Lighting`, `DeferredLighting`, `PostProcess`, `HistoryCopy`, `Overlay`, and `UI`; the readback path emits `Readback` before the GPU-to-CPU copy. Graph-stage execution maps `RenderPassStage::Lighting` to the generic `zircon::Lighting` marker so Forward+ lighting stages are not mislabeled as deferred, while the fixed deferred lighting pass still uses `zircon::DeferredLighting`. Each executed graph pass now also emits and records `zircon::RenderGraphPass::<pass-name>`, so compute-declared passes that fall back to the graphics queue still leave pass-level capture evidence and `RenderStats.last_graph_executed_debug_markers` can prove marker coverage without exposing WGPU encoder internals.

For Windows RenderDoc capture, launch Zircon from RenderDoc with environment variables set before process start: use `WGPU_BACKEND=dx12` for Direct3D 12 or `WGPU_BACKEND=vulkan` for Vulkan, set `WGPU_DEBUG=1` and `WGPU_VALIDATION=1` when validation output is needed, and set `ZR_RENDERDOC_CAPTURE_NEXT=1` to capture the first created viewport's next submit. After capture, inspect the event browser for `zircon::FrameExtract`, `zircon::MainScene`, `zircon::Lighting`, `zircon::PostProcess`, `zircon::HistoryCopy`, and `zircon::UI`; temporal TAA evidence should appear through the `temporal.taa-resolve` graph pass and the `taa.history.previous.scene-color`, `taa.history.current.scene-color`, and `taa.output.scene-color` resources, while non-TAA history textures remain labeled `zircon-history-global-illumination`, `zircon-history-ambient-occlusion`, and the dedicated SSR history target. The CPU fallback path still marks final readback as `zircon::Readback`, while the app-host window path now binds a native surface and finishes redraw through a wgpu swapchain `SurfaceTexture::present()` after a `zircon-present-blit-pass`. Do not reintroduce legacy `HistoryResolve`; temporal scene-color blending belongs to the TAA path.

The cargo wrapper attempted first still timed out during lib-test compile/link and is not counted as passing; product/RenderDoc acceptance remains open.

The 2026-06-21 LS-M3 directional shadow-atlas capture slice added `render_product_directional_shadow_atlas_capture_records_receiver_path` and `render_product_directional_shadow_atlas_darkens_receiver_capture`, now owned by `render_product_shadow_captures/directional.rs`. The 2026-06-21 LS-M3 CSM stability guard adds `render_product_csm_directional_remains_stable_under_subtexel_camera_shift`, now owned by `render_product_shadow_captures/directional.rs`. The guard submits the same directional receiver/caster scene in baseline and x=0.006 subtexel-shifted camera positions, renders shadowed and unshadowed variants for each camera, then compares per-camera darkened-pixel and luma-delta statistics so ordinary camera projection movement is cancelled by the matching unshadowed baseline. The 2026-06-21 LS-M3 forward/deferred shadow parity guard adds `render_product_directional_shadow_atlas_forward_deferred_darkening_parity`, now owned by `render_product_shadow_captures/directional.rs`. The first red run proved the gap with Deferred reporting `pixels=0` and `sum=0.00` for the shadowed/unshadowed delta while Forward+ darkened normally; the root cause was that Deferred G-buffer material alpha stored only the shading model and dropped the receiver `receive_shadows` flag. `deferred_geometry.wgsl` now encodes low 7 bits as shading model plus high bit as receive-shadow policy, and `deferred_lighting.wgsl` decodes that flag before sampling `zr_gpu_light_shadow_visibility(...)`. Validation passed the exact parity Cargo filter with 1/1 after the fix, plus `deferred_geometry_shader` 7/7, `deferred_lighting_shader` 6/6, and `deferred_material_gbuffer_shaders_encode_and_decode_material_channels` 1/1 in `target\codex-runtime-shadow-spot-0621` with the existing warning set.

The 2026-06-21 LS-M4 PCF quality guard adds `render_product_spot_shadow_pcf_quality_changes_receiver_edge_capture` in `render_product_shadow_captures.rs`. The guard submits Low, High, and unshadowed baseline frames for a spot receiver/caster edge scene, then requires measurable receiver darkening in both PCF modes plus an RGB capture delta between Low and High. The earlier directional PCF scene produced identical Low/High captures; a temporary diagnostic proved the High flag reached `zr_shadow.wgsl`, and the accepted path uses the spot edge scene with the High 9-tap kernel radius widened to 8 texels. Validation passed the exact PCF Cargo filter 1/1 and the `shadow_atlas_resources` source-contract filter 3/3 in `target\codex-runtime-shadow-spot-0621` with the existing warning set.

The 2026-06-05 SSR ray-march slice replaced the old one-sample reflected-coordinate seed with a bounded final-pass screen-space march over the existing depth/normal/material resources. The shader now caps SSR marching to 128 in-shader steps, uses authored max-step/max-distance/thickness settings, depth-tests candidate samples, fades by ray distance and screen edge, and keeps roughness attenuation. The 2026-06-05 SSR projection follow-up added `PostProcessParams.effect_projection` as an internal WGPU uniform vector derived from the current camera and viewport. The shader now reconstructs the current pixel's view-space origin from camera-linearized depth, uses the view vector for reflection, marches in view-space distance, projects each candidate back to screen pixels, and keeps the same bounded depth/thickness/roughness/edge gates. Scoped `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check`, standalone raw/fallback Naga WGSL validation for `post_process.wgsl`, `cargo check -p zircon_runtime --lib --tests --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-render-main-chain --message-format short --color never`, focused `cargo test -p zircon_runtime --lib effect_stack_settings_are_encoded_into_post_process_params --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-render-main-chain --message-format short --color never -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture`, and focused `cargo test -p zircon_runtime --lib post_process_shader_ray_marches_ssr_with_bounds_and_edge_fade --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-render-main-chain --message-format short --color never -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture` passed with existing warnings only.

The 2026-06-14 TP-M2-S2 jitter audit follow-up keeps that SSR projection contract explicitly unjittered. `build_post_process_params(...)` now has a regression proving `temporal_jitter` does not change `effect_projection` or `effect_view_x/y/z`, and reflection probe screen projection now reads `ViewProjectionMatrixPair::clip_from_world_unjittered` through the post-process camera helper. The local duplicate perspective/orthographic helper files under `camera_matrices/` were deleted so post-process projection cannot drift from the shared camera matrix pair. `SceneUniform::inverse_view_proj` was also switched to the inverse of `view_proj_unjittered`, which keeps deferred lighting world-position reconstruction stable under TAA jitter. Validation passed `cargo fmt --package zircon_runtime -- --check` and `cargo check -p zircon_runtime --lib --no-default-features --features core-min --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir D:\cargo-targets\zircon-runtime-temporal-s2-0614 --message-format short --color never` with the existing warning set.

The 2026-06-14 TP-M2-S3 TAA-off product baseline adds `render_product_temporal_off_matches_anti_alias_feature_disabled_product` to `render_product_anti_alias.rs`. The test submits the same `World::new()` extract with `AntiAliasSettings::off()` through two WGPU product paths: AA feature enabled but requested Off, and a quality profile with AA feature disabled. It asserts both paths resolve requested/effective AA mode to Off, execute no AA/FXAA product pass, and produce identical captured RGBA bytes. This is the current in-repository product baseline for "TAA/AA off does not change output". The 2026-06-15 artifact audit checked current docs/tests/examples and limited git history, including `0559abbc`, and found only the original plan text for `render_product_temporal_off_matches_pre_jitter_baseline`, not a recoverable test implementation or hash artifact. A historical hash can still be added later as external supplemental evidence, but it is not a current repository blocker. Validation passed `cargo fmt --package zircon_runtime` and the scoped core-min library check in `D:\cargo-targets\zircon-runtime-temporal-s3-0614`. The focused lib-test build is still blocked by unrelated UI table input diagnostics fields, not by render product code.

The 2026-06-05 SSR hit-refinement follow-up kept the then-current SSR path bounded and improved the accepted hit. `post_process.wgsl` shared the depth/thickness/distance/edge visibility calculation between the coarse march and a four-step `refine_screen_space_reflection_hit(...)` interval search, then sampled the refined coordinate before blending. The later SSR resolve split now runs that same helper family from `fs_screen_space_reflection_resolve`. The raw and viewport-depth-fallback post-process shader tests ran Naga validation. `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check zircon_runtime/src/graphics/scene/scene_renderer/post_process/resources/new/create_pipeline_bundle/post_process_pipeline.rs` passed. `cargo test -p zircon_runtime --lib post_process_shader --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-render-main-chain --message-format short --color never -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture` passed 8 shader tests, and `cargo test -p zircon_runtime --lib post_process_viewport_depth_fallback_shader_parses_for_gl_backends --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-render-main-chain --message-format short --color never -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture` passed the fallback shader regression. `cargo check -p zircon_runtime --lib --tests --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-render-main-chain --message-format short --color never` passed with existing warnings only.

The 2026-06-05 SSR normal-space follow-up keeps `gbuffer-normal` as a world-space graph resource for the existing normal prepass/deferred contract, but adds postprocess-only camera view-basis rows to `PostProcessParams`. The SSR helper shader converts the sampled world normal through `effect_view_x/y/z` before reflecting the reconstructed view-space ray. Scoped `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check` passed over the touched postprocess Rust files. `cargo test -p zircon_runtime --lib camera_view_basis_is_encoded_for_post_process_normals --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-render-main-chain --message-format short --color never -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture` passed the rotated-camera uniform regression with existing warnings only. Direct execution of `E:\cargo-targets\zircon-render-main-chain\debug\deps\zircon_runtime-b34ee8d8fc52f1fd.exe post_process_shader --test-threads=1 --nocapture` passed 8 shader source-contract tests including raw Naga validation and the updated SSR normal contract, and the same binary passed `post_process_viewport_depth_fallback_shader_parses_for_gl_backends --test-threads=1 --nocapture`.

The 2026-06-05 SSR temporal-history follow-up first used previous scene-color history and the reconstructed motion-vector neighbor-max target inside the then-current composition path. The later SSR history-feedback and resolve slices replaced that with dedicated `history.previous.screen-space-reflection` input and a graph-visible resolve writer. The current shader still reprojects history with current-minus-previous velocity, rejects unavailable/offscreen history, clamps reused history to the current 3x3 scene-color neighborhood, and blends it into the refined SSR result with a visibility/roughness/motion-stability bounded weight before writing the resolved history target.

The 2026-06-05 postprocess shader-source split moved the SSR helper family into `post_process_screen_space_reflection.wgsl`. `resources/shader_sources.rs` is now the single owner of the assembled `POST_PROCESS_SHADER`, so production final/resolve pipeline creation, raw shader source-contract tests, and viewport-depth fallback rewrites all consume the same base-plus-SSR source. Validation passed scoped `rustfmt --edition 2021`, scoped `rustfmt --check`, source scans for base/SSR helper ownership and assembled-source routing, conflict-marker and trailing-whitespace scans, and path-scoped `git diff --check` with expected LF/CRLF warnings only. Clean Cargo/Naga validation is not claimed for this split because unrelated Cargo/Rust compiler lanes were active and no standalone `naga`, `wgsl_analyzer`, or `wgpu-info` command was installed.

The 2026-06-05 SSR specular-occlusion follow-up originally added AO-backed attenuation inside the SSR helper family by sampling the already-bound `ambient_occlusion_tex`. That inline helper has since been superseded by the graph-visible `screen-space-reflection-specular-occlusion` pass: the pass stores AO plus material/depth response factors in `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.specular-occlusion`, and the split resolve samples that target at binding 22 before multiplying by live traced-hit visibility. The earlier slice remains relevant as the formula origin, but the current product path no longer keeps the attenuation as a final-stack-only helper. Validation for the earlier slice passed scoped `rustfmt --edition 2021`, scoped `rustfmt --check`, shader/test/docs source scans, conflict-marker and trailing-whitespace scans, and path-scoped `git diff --check` with expected line-ending warnings only. No Cargo/Naga validation was claimed because unrelated Cargo/Rust compiler lanes were active and no standalone WGSL validator was installed.

The 2026-06-05 SSR history-target contract follow-up reserved the graph-visible target later used by the split resolve path. `PostProcessGraphResourceNames` now includes `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.history`; active SSR resolve/final nodes route through it; and the compiled graph materializes it as a renderer-owned transient texture so product-node validation no longer relies on an implicit or fake resource.

The 2026-06-05 SSR history-writer follow-up made the dedicated target concrete at the WGPU pass boundary, and the later split resolve follow-up moved that write out of final composition. The current production descriptor and plugin fixture declare `screen-space-reflection-resolve` as the graph transient writer for `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.history`; final `post-process` reads that target and writes only final composited/color plus global illumination. The old final-writer shape is superseded, but the target resource remains part of the accepted graph-owned path.

The 2026-06-05 SSR history-feedback follow-up gives SSR a dedicated previous-frame source. `SceneFrameHistoryTextures` now stores a screen-space-reflection texture, renderer history allocation/copy includes it when SSR feedback is active, valid history imports it as `history.previous.screen-space-reflection`, binding 20 exposes it as `history_screen_space_reflection_tex`, and the SSR shader reprojects/clamps that history while preserving alpha as feedback confidence.

The 2026-06-05 graph execution-context split keeps the common GPU context focused while moving postprocess GPU bridge code out of the 990-line `render_pass_execution_context/gpu.rs` warning band. `gpu.rs` now stays around the shared renderer payload and scene/non-postprocess record helpers; `gpu/post_process.rs` owns `RenderPassPostProcessStackContext`, `record_post_process_stack(...)`, SSAO, clustered lighting, bloom, DoF prepare, motion-vector clear/camera/tile/neighbor passes, split SSR resolve, and their compute-dispatch bookkeeping. The 2026-06-17 Plan 01 resolver migration also moved deferred G-buffer/deferred lighting bridge methods into `gpu/deferred.rs`, sprite/preview-sky/UI/overlay bridge methods into `gpu/surface.rs`, and particle transparent/velocity bridge lookup through `gpu/particle.rs`, keeping `gpu.rs` under the local modularization threshold while those paths migrate onto resolver-backed resource lookup.

The 2026-06-05 SSR resolve split follow-up adds `screen-space-reflection-resolve` / `post.screen-space-reflection-resolve` as the concrete writer for `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.history`. The pass reads scene color/depth, G-buffer normal/material, `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.specular-occlusion`, reconstructed motion vectors, and optional previous SSR history; the resolve pipeline runs `fs_screen_space_reflection_resolve`; and final `post.stack` samples the resolved target at binding 21 instead of computing and writing SSR history itself. Binding 20 remains previous-history feedback for the resolve pass. The resolve executor binds binding 21 to fallback while writing the SSR target to avoid a sampled-texture/color-attachment conflict in WGPU.

The 2026-06-05 SSR specular-occlusion split follow-up adds `screen-space-reflection-specular-occlusion` / `post.screen-space-reflection-specular-occlusion` before resolve. The pass reads `scene-depth`, `gbuffer-material`, and `ambient-occlusion`, writes graph-owned `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.specular-occlusion` as a frame-size `Rgba8Unorm` transient, and exposes that target to the shared postprocess bind group at binding 22. The shader stores AO plus material/depth response factors in the split target; resolve multiplies the sampled factor by the live traced-hit visibility, preserving hit rejection while making the attenuation product visible to graph/product validation. Final `post.stack` still reads AO directly for ambient occlusion and reads the resolved SSR history at binding 21.

The 2026-06-05 SSR depth-pyramid follow-up adds `screen-space-reflection-depth-pyramid` / `post.screen-space-reflection-depth-pyramid` before specular occlusion and resolve. The pass reads `scene-depth`, writes graph-owned `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.depth-pyramid` as a half-resolution `Rgba16Float` transient, and exposes that target to the shared postprocess bind group at binding 23. The shader stores min/max linear view depth from each 2x2 source-depth cell; resolve samples that range and reduces hit visibility when the ray depth falls outside the coarse cell range. This is first-level SSR range gating only, not a full hierarchical depth/reflection pyramid.

The 2026-06-05 SSR reflection-pyramid follow-up adds `screen-space-reflection-reflection-pyramid` / `post.screen-space-reflection-reflection-pyramid` before specular occlusion and resolve. The pass reads `scene-color`, writes graph-owned `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.reflection-pyramid` as a half-resolution `Rgba16Float` transient, and exposes that target to the shared postprocess bind group at binding 24. The shader stores a 2x2 scene-color average; resolve samples that coarse color and blends rougher ray hits toward it while sharper hits continue to use direct scene-color sampling. This is first-level SSR color reuse only, not a full multi-mip reflection pyramid.

The 2026-06-05 SSR coarse-pyramid follow-up adds `screen-space-reflection-depth-pyramid-coarse` and `screen-space-reflection-reflection-pyramid-coarse` between the first-level pyramid passes and resolve. The depth coarse pass downsamples first-level min/max ranges into `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.depth-pyramid.coarse` at binding 25; the reflection coarse pass downsamples first-level color into `postprocess.screen-space-reflection.reflection-pyramid.coarse` at binding 26. Resolve combines first-level and coarse depth-range gating and blends rough surfaces toward first-level then coarse reflection color. The remaining gap is a dynamic multi-mip SSR hierarchy, not the fixed second level now wired here.

The 2026-06-05 SSR bridge modularization is behavior-preserving submit plumbing. `render_pass_execution_context/gpu/post_process/screen_space_reflection.rs` now owns the WGPU graph bridge methods for SSR resolve, split specular occlusion, first-level depth/reflection pyramid, and coarse depth/reflection pyramid passes. The 2026-06-17 resolver follow-up migrates those SSR-specific graph texture inputs/outputs, optional fallback slots, mip target alias lookup, and mip-count metadata through `gpu/resource_lookup.rs`. The subsequent root postprocess resolver slice moves the broader stack/color-LUT/effect-chain/compute-resource/temporal/terminal bridge lookups through the same helper boundary and splits the large root bridge into `effects.rs`, `computed_resources.rs`, `temporal.rs`, and `terminal.rs`.
The 2026-06-24 SSR GPU context test owner split keeps that production bridge in `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/graph_execution/render_pass_execution_context/gpu/post_process/screen_space_reflection.rs` while moving private parent-pyramid mip tests to `graphics/scene/scene_renderer/graph_execution/render_pass_execution_context/gpu/post_process/screen_space_reflection/tests.rs`. Guard `runtime_15_ssr_gpu_context_tests_are_child_owner_split` locks the parent/test child shape and docs/status anchors under `render_plan07_ssr_gpu_context_test_owner_split_static_passed_cargo_deferred_active_compile_lane`; the slice has scoped rustfmt/static/line-count/docs-anchor/whitespace/diff-check evidence only, with Cargo/WGPU/RenderDoc deferred because active compile lanes were present.

The 2026-06-05 SSR motion-vector execution-gate follow-up makes the concrete WGPU producer gate match the graph/resource contract. `render_pass_execution_context/gpu/post_process.rs` now requests reconstructed motion vectors when either motion blur or screen-space reflection is active, and the focused unit coverage proves the helper is true for both temporal families and false for the default stack. This is an execution bridge fix only: no new object/skinned/particle velocity writer, full dynamic velocity pyramid, or full hierarchical SSR reflection/depth pyramid is claimed.

The 2026-06-11 optional post-process executor gate moves the same enablement policy to the graph executor entry points after a Vulkan RenderDoc capture of `examples/v.rdc` showed the vampire scene running motion-vector, DoF prepare, and SSR pyramid/resolve passes even though the scene-authored effect stack did not enable those families. `builtin_postprocess_executors.rs` now reads the submitted frame's `RenderPostProcessEffectStackSettings` before resource lookup and skips the motion-vector chain unless motion blur or SSR is enabled, skips `depth-of-field-prepare` unless DoF is enabled, and skips every SSR pyramid/specular/resolve executor unless SSR is enabled. This is an execution-cost reduction only: the compiled graph can still contain the optional pass names for product ordering and diagnostics, but disabled families no longer create render passes, bind resources, build SSR mip loops, or request motion-vector camera status. `render_framework_skips_advanced_postprocess_work_when_effects_are_disabled` covers the default submit path, `render_framework_submits_advanced_postprocess_graph_passes` keeps the enabled path alive, and `optional_postprocess_executors_skip_resource_work_when_effects_are_disabled` covers executor-level skipping. Validation on Windows passed `cargo test -p zircon_runtime --lib render_pass_executor_registry -- --nocapture`, the two focused post-process submit tests, and `cargo fmt --check`; existing unrelated warning output remains.

The 2026-06-11 model-uniform cache follow-up targets the same vampire RenderDoc capture's remaining CPU/GPU submission churn: the capture showed 230 draws and 231 pre-draw `vkCmdCopyBuffer()` calls, with many 176-byte `zircon-model-buffer` resources matching the per-draw `ModelUniform` allocation path. Prepared non-skinned mesh draws now build a stable `ModelUniformCacheKey` from entity, mesh or model primitive, material, selected LOD, and the exact raster slice index range, then reuse the cached `zircon-model-bind-group` and `zircon-model-buffer` across frames. When a transform, tint, shadow receiver flag, previous-model matrix, or normal-texture flag changes, `ModelUniformCache` updates the existing buffer with `queue.write_buffer(...)`; dynamic and skinned draws keep the uncached path because their joint-palette bindings and dynamic geometry can change per frame. `SceneRendererCore` owns the generation-scoped cache for both compiled and direct render paths and drops entries not touched in the current frame, so missing draws do not accumulate unbounded GPU resources. Validation passed `cargo check -p zircon_runtime --lib --message-format short --color never`, `cargo test -p zircon_runtime --lib model_uniform_cache -- --nocapture`, and the two focused optional-postprocess tests with existing unrelated warnings only. This does not claim a measured FPS result yet; the app must be rerun and recaptured to verify that the steady-state `zircon-model-buffer` creation/copy count drops in a fresh `.rdc`.

The 2026-06-11 material-uniform cache follow-up addresses the other small-buffer pattern in the loaded capture: repeated `zircon-material-property-uniform-buffer` and `zircon-standard-material-uniform-buffer` resources beside WGPU staging buffers. `ResourceStreamer::ensure_material(...)` now keys prepared materials by the asset revision returned from the resource registry and returns early when the material is already prepared for the same revision, so `ensure_scene_resources(...)` can visit the same material through many mesh snapshots without recreating the property uniform, standard uniform, and material bind groups. Missing-material fallback keeps its previous behavior by caching under a `None` revision key; if a real resource record later appears, the key changes to `Some(revision)` and the material is rebuilt from the registered asset. `render_product_streamer_reuses_material_uniforms_for_unchanged_revision` covers property and standard uniform `Arc` reuse for unchanged material revisions, while `render_product_streamer_repeated_blocking_material_ensure_remains_blocking` and `render_product_pbr_streamer_records_missing_material_fallback_runtime` guard the cached error and fallback paths. Focused execution through the freshly built lib-test binary in `D:\cargo-targets\zircon-vampire-perf-0611\debug\deps` passed all three checks on 2026-06-11.

The 2026-06-07 effect-stack stylistic contract follow-up does not add a new renderer pass; it locks the existing final-pass style lanes to the submit/WGPU boundary. `build_post_process_params(...)` focused coverage now verifies vignette intensity/smoothness/roundness, film-grain intensity/response, dither intensity/scale, chromatic-aberration intensity/spread, and fog density/height/color packing into the four effect uniform vectors. `post_process_pipeline.rs` now has an assembled-shader source contract for the matching WGSL fields and helper chain, including chromatic aberration before resolved SSR composition and fog/vignette/grain-dither afterward. The reference basis remains Unity Graphics for post-processing override families and Bevy for the Rust/WGPU fullscreen-pass setting upload shape. Validation passed scoped rustfmt check, source scans for the new CPU/shader assertions, direct WGSL final-stack order scanning, conflict-marker and trailing-whitespace scans, and path-scoped `git diff --check` with expected LF/CRLF warnings only; no Cargo command was started because unrelated Cargo processes were already active in the checkout. This keeps the current claim narrow: no physical film-grain texture path, aerial-perspective model, per-effect graph pass split, or editor authoring UI is delivered by this slice.

The paired diagnostics follow-up extends the public stats evidence for the same family list. `effect_stack_report_records_active_approximated_and_missing_resources` and `render_framework_stats_report_effect_stack_product_node_when_authored` now cover `vignette`, `film-grain`, `dither`, `chromatic-aberration`, and `fog` as active families, while the approximation report remains limited to the heavier `depth-of-field`, `motion-blur`, and `screen-space-reflection` families.

The same contract coverage now reaches the volume authoring path through `VolumeEvaluator`. Evaluator tests assert global priority blending, box and sphere influence, camera volume-mask filtering, preserved unset override parameters, and unknown-component errors before submit builds the resolved `PostProcessExtract`; no new editor UI or per-effect pass policy is introduced by that test expansion.

The neutral settings now also own style-family enablement. `RenderPostProcessEffectStackSettings::is_enabled()` and `RenderPostProcessEffectStackReport` call `is_enabled()` on vignette, film grain, dither, chromatic aberration, and fog settings, so submit/product-node activation and public active-family reporting share the same predicates instead of duplicating raw intensity or density checks.

The 2026-06-07 renderer-upload cleanup extends that ownership to style-family upload sanitization. The final WGPU parameter packer now calls settings-owned `render_*` helpers from `effect_stack_settings/style_settings.rs` for vignette intensity/smoothness/roundness, film-grain intensity/response, dither intensity/scale, chromatic-aberration intensity/sample-spread, and fog density/height/color. This is a boundary cleanup only: the final pass still consumes one packed effect stack, and no new per-effect graph executor or UI authoring surface is introduced.

The same cleanup now covers DoF, motion blur, and SSR upload policy. `effect_stack_settings/depth_of_field_settings.rs`, `motion_blur_settings.rs`, and `screen_space_reflection_settings.rs` own the clamped renderer values for DoF focus/aperture/radius/lens fields, motion-blur shutter/sample fields, and SSR intensity/thickness/ray-distance/temporal/roughness fields. `build_post_process_params(...)` delegates to those helpers, so submit-side settings remain the single CPU upload contract while the final pass, pass graph, and shader bindings stay unchanged.

The color-transform upload path uses the same settings-owned boundary. `effect_stack_settings/color_transform_settings.rs` now owns tonemap operator encoding, white-point clamping, LUT intensity clamping, and LUT layout validation, and `build_post_process_params(...)` reads those helpers for `effect_flags` plus `effect_tonemap_lut`. This does not change the final pass layout or LUT residency path; it removes duplicated upload policy from the WGPU parameter packer.

The scalar blur upload path is also settings-owned. `effect_stack_settings/blur_settings.rs` now owns `RenderBlurSettings::is_enabled()` and `render_radius()`, so submit/product-node activation and `effect_blur_dof` packing share the same neutral settings contract.

Effect-stack reporting is now split from the aggregate settings declaration as well. `effect_stack_settings/report.rs` owns the active-family, approximated-family, and missing-resource report behavior; `effect_stack_settings/resource_status.rs` owns the renderer readiness DTO consumed by submit stats. The public re-export names remain unchanged for submit callers.

The 2026-06-05 DoF lens/bokeh follow-up extends the neutral DoF settings and final-pass postprocess shader without claiming the full Unity-style CoC pipeline. `RenderDepthOfFieldSettings` now carries focus range, focal length, bokeh blade count, and bokeh rotation; volume resolution blends continuous lens values and treats blade count as discrete; `PostProcessParams.effect_dof_lens` uploads sanitized values; and `post_process.wgsl` uses a bounded rotated disk/bokeh kernel instead of the old four-direction cross blur. Validation used the isolated `E:\cargo-targets\zircon-dof-lens-0605` target dir after the shared render target hit a stale fingerprint path error. `cargo check -p zircon_runtime --lib --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-dof-lens-0605 --message-format short --color never` passed with existing warnings only. The first Cargo test wrapper timed out during Windows test-harness build/link, then the generated binary completed the focused direct runs: `effect_stack` passed 23 tests, `depth_of_field` passed 2 tests, `post_process_shader` passed 9 shader/source-contract tests with raw Naga validation, and `post_process_viewport_depth_fallback_shader_parses_for_gl_backends` passed 1 fallback shader regression. Scoped rustfmt, `git diff --check`, and conflict-marker scans passed with only expected line-ending warnings.

The 2026-06-05 DoF resource-contract follow-up reserves the split-pass resource vocabulary without claiming the finished physical DoF pass set. `PostProcessGraphResourceNames` now includes `postprocess.depth-of-field.coc` and `postprocess.depth-of-field.bokeh`; active DoF effect-stack nodes produce those outputs; and the current graph contract materializes them as graph-owned transient scratch textures from compiled descriptors rather than importing fixed `OffscreenTarget` aliases. The first `depth-of-field-prepare` follow-up adds a graph-visible SRP pass and registered executor that requires the graph `scene-depth` input and writes to the CoC and bokeh scratch views before `post.stack`. The CoC-prepare continuation replaces the CoC clear with `depth_of_field_prepare.wgsl`, `DepthOfFieldPrepareParams`, a DoF prepare bind group/layout/pipeline, and GL/WebGL/ANGLE viewport-depth fallback rewrite support; it writes signed near/far normalized CoC into the CoC scratch target. The later bokeh prefilter continuation moves the bokeh target beyond clear-only ownership: `depth-of-field-prepare` now reads graph `scene-color`, writes a CoC-weighted center-plus-four-neighbor bokeh prefilter seed into the bokeh scratch target, and final postprocess binds CoC plus bokeh scratch at bindings 17/18 while preserving the scene-depth-derived fallback. For the CoC and bokeh consumption slices, scoped rustfmt checks, conflict-marker scans, trailing-whitespace scans, and path-scoped `git diff --check` passed with only expected line-ending warnings. Clean Cargo validation for CoC consumption is not claimed: focused `post_process_shader_samples_bound_depth_of_field_coc_texture` Cargo test runs timed out without compiler or test output in both a fresh target dir and the warmed render-main-chain target dir, and no scoped Cargo/Rust process remained afterward. Clean Cargo validation for the CoC-prepare continuation is also not claimed: the focused `depth_of_field_prepare` Cargo test first exited without a usable diagnostic after dependency compilation and then timed out on rerun, while scoped `cargo check -p zircon_runtime --lib --locked --jobs 1` timed out in both fresh and warmed target dirs before stale scoped wrappers were stopped.

The 2026-06-05 DoF bokeh prefilter follow-up moves the bokeh scratch target from clear-only to a concrete seed/prefilter handoff. The built-in and plugin-fixture post-process descriptors now make `depth-of-field-prepare` read `scene-color` and make `post-process` read both DoF scratch resources. The prepare shader binds scene color at binding 2 and writes a CoC-weighted center-plus-four-neighbor bokeh prefilter seed into `postprocess.depth-of-field.bokeh`; the final shader binds that target at binding 18 and samples that prefiltered seed through the bounded bokeh kernel before mixing with the existing procedural final-pass bokeh path. The next final-pass weighting/dilation continuation uses the prepared CoC far/near channels while sampling bokeh: far-field samples are gated by the smaller center/sample far CoC, near-field samples can contribute from the sample's foreground CoC, and the final radius/mix decision uses a center-plus-four-neighbor max CoC dilation. This is not yet half-resolution bokeh simulation, temporal CoC history, a dedicated near/far combine pass, or a dedicated postfilter/combine pass. Validation for the seed/prefilter and weighting follow-ups passed scoped rustfmt, conflict-marker and trailing-whitespace scans, and path-scoped `git diff --check` with expected line-ending warnings only. Clean Cargo validation is not claimed for the final-pass weighting continuation: `cargo test -p zircon_runtime --lib post_process_shader_samples_bound_depth_of_field_bokeh_texture --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-dof-near-far-0605 --message-format short --color never -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture` timed out without compiler or test output; matching Cargo/Rust processes were stopped and no matching validation process remained afterward.

The 2026-06-05 motion-vector target-contract follow-up reserves the first concrete motion-vector frame target without claiming complete motion blur. `PostProcessGraphResourceNames` originally introduced `scene-motion-vector`; the Plan 06 temporal cutover now uses `SCENE_VELOCITY = "scene-velocity"` for the raw graph target while tile/coarse/neighbor reconstruction keeps the existing postprocess resource names. The follow-up motion-blur authoring slice adds `RenderMotionBlurSettings`, volume blending, active/approximation report labels, graph `scene-depth` plus velocity input, and the missing `effect-stack.motion-blur.velocity-prepass` diagnostic. The old clear/camera/object post-process producers were replaced by the Temporal feature's `velocity-object` and `velocity-camera` passes. Camera history is still stored as a previous-camera snapshot on the viewport record after successful submissions and carried into `ViewportRenderFrame`; object previous transforms now come only from GPUScene's rolled `prev_world_from_local` state, not from a CPU viewport object-history map. Skinned GPU velocity consumes GPUScene's successful-submit previous skinned-palette roll when skeleton signature/joint count still match. CPU-morphed GPU-skinning sources carry a morph-shape signature; when it matches the previous frame the current morphed source is reused as previous shape, and when it changes the draw uses GPUScene's rolled previous source mesh through the velocity pipeline's previous-position vertex slot. Missing signatures or missing previous sources report `render.mesh.queue.skinned_gpu_cpu_morphed_previous_shape_velocity_missing_count` instead of the generic missing velocity transform count. This is deliberately separate from temporal color history: `FrameHistoryValidationKey` may still invalidate history on camera movement while the velocity producers compare against previous submitted camera/GPUScene state. The submit stats path now treats `effect-stack.motion-blur.velocity-prepass` as present when `temporal.velocity-camera`, `temporal.velocity-object`, `post.motion-vector-tile-max`, `post.motion-vector-tile-max-coarse`, and `post.motion-vector-neighbor-max` executed; it still reports the gap when any pass is absent. This is still not complete motion blur because broader particle/custom-shader velocity coverage, a full dynamic multi-level tile-max/neighbor-max pyramid, dedicated velocity-depth GPU readback coverage, richer camera-cut policy, and RenderDoc acceptance are still open.

The 2026-06-15 particle velocity writer follow-up adds the first concrete particle producer for that raw target. Plugin descriptors may declare `particle.velocity`, the built-in executor records through `RenderPassGpuExecutionContext::record_particle_velocity_to_resource(...)`, and `ParticleRenderer::record_velocity(...)` expands matched current/previous particle billboard rows into paired vertices for `particle_velocity.wgsl`. The shader uses unjittered current and previous scene matrices and writes xy screen velocity into `scene-velocity`; unmatched sprites are skipped and remain visible in `RenderStats.last_particle_velocity_missing_sprite_count`. The stable identity follow-up adds `RenderParticleSpriteIdentity { entity, stable_sprite_key }`, copies the key through `RenderParticlePreviousSpriteSnapshot::from_current(...)`, parses `stable_sprite_key`/`sprite_key` from current JSON sprites, and makes both previous-state stats and the velocity writer match by identity instead of entity-only rows. The renderer-owned previous-state follow-up stores the current frame's particle snapshots on `ViewportRecord` after successful submit/present/direct runtime-frame paths and injects those rows through `FrameSubmissionContext` when an extract does not supply explicit previous rows, so a second particle frame can write velocity without authored previous-state data. The previous-basis follow-up adds optional `RenderParticleBillboardBasisSnapshot`, records the submitted camera right/up basis into renderer-owned previous rows, and makes `build_particle_velocity_vertices(...)` use that basis for previous corners while preserving current-basis fallback for explicit legacy rows. The readback follow-up adds `RenderSceneVelocityReadbackReport` and a test-build readback from the graph-owned `Rg16Float` `scene-velocity` surface, with `render_product_particle_velocity_writer_writes_nonzero_scene_velocity_pixels` proving a matched particle frame produces nonzero raw velocity pixels. The renderer-owned previous-state product test now also asserts the second moved frame produces nonzero raw `scene-velocity` pixels from viewport-owned previous rows, the same-entity keyed product baseline proves two nonzero-key sprites keep missing count at zero and still write nonzero raw velocity on the renderer-owned second frame, and the three-frame keyed baseline proves four same-entity nonzero-key sprites continue rolling renderer-owned previous rows and writing nonzero raw velocity across consecutive moved frames. The 32-sprite keyed stress-field product test extends that same previous-row handoff to a broader same-entity list, with first-frame missing=32/ambiguity=0 and second-frame missing=0/ambiguity=0 plus nonzero readback. The key `0` policy follow-up keeps single-sprite anonymous compatibility but moves the previous-state policy into `frame_extract/particle_extract_policy.rs`, reports same-entity key `0` multi-sprite streams through `last_particle_velocity_anonymous_stream_ambiguity_count` and `render.particle.velocity.anonymous_stream_ambiguity_count`, excludes those rows from previous-state matching and velocity vertices, and prevents renderer-owned previous cache from carrying them forward. `World::collect_render_particles(...)` now migrates generated world-HUD bar background/fill sprites to nonzero stable keys. The focused product group now passes 9 filtered tests, including the two-frame key `0` hard-reject baseline. This is still V1 coverage: RenderDoc evidence remains follow-up work.

The 2026-06-14 Plan 06 TP-M2-S1b follow-up connects the TAA jitter state to that same submit boundary. `FrameSubmissionContext` resolves effective anti-aliasing first, then emits a nonzero Halton jitter only for effective TAA; Off/Fxaa/Msaa and TAA fallback modes write a zero `TemporalJitterSample`. `build_runtime_frame(...)` and direct `submit_runtime_frame(...)` both project the effective anti_alias settings and jitter into the `ViewportRenderFrame` extract before WGPU execution. The 2026-06-18 Plan 09 runtime-frame cutover keeps that jitter on the selected `CameraRenderDescriptor` as well: `ViewportRenderFrame::camera()` returns the selected descriptor, while scene uniforms, shadow cascades, particle billboards, depth-of-field preparation, velocity-camera post-process, and previous-camera history call `effective_camera()` when they still need a projected `ViewportCameraSnapshot`. Successful submit/present paths store that projected selected camera with jitter cleared and advance `ViewportRecord.temporal_frame_index`, so camera/background velocity and object velocity read unjittered current/previous matrices while rasterization can use the jittered current `view_proj`. `SceneUniform` now carries `view_proj`, `view_proj_unjittered`, `previous_view_proj_unjittered`, and `jitter_params`; fallback mesh, deferred lighting, HZB occlusion cull, and builtin PBR shader layouts are updated to the same ABI.

The 2026-06-05 motion-vector camera-cut gate follow-up adds deterministic previous-camera reuse checks to `VelocityCameraParams`. Large translation relative to the shared far-plane range and rotations beyond the local 60-degree threshold now disable `temporal.velocity-camera` for that frame, leaving the clear-stored `scene-velocity` target neutral instead of blurring a camera cut. This closes the coarse translation/rotation cut gate while keeping richer temporal policy, skinned/particle velocity ownership, full multi-level tile-max/neighbor-max pyramid reconstruction, and GPU-visible velocity/depth coverage open.

The 2026-06-05 motion-vector projection-shape gate follow-up extends that deterministic reuse policy to projection parameter jumps. Large perspective FOV changes, large orthographic-size changes, and large near/far clip-plane changes now disable `temporal.velocity-camera` for the frame, so projection cuts use the same neutral-clear path as translation/rotation cuts. Skinned/particle velocity ownership, richer temporal policy, full multi-level tile-max/neighbor-max pyramid reconstruction, and GPU-visible velocity/depth coverage remain open.

The 2026-06-05 motion-vector invalid-camera guard follow-up keeps the camera/background producer's disabled uniform finite. `VelocityCameraParams` now validates FOV and clip ranges before generating matrices and falls back to identity current/previous clip matrices if any generated matrix is non-finite, so invalid camera snapshots leave `scene-velocity` neutral instead of uploading NaN reprojection data. Validation passed scoped rustfmt/check, source-contract scans, conflict-marker and trailing-whitespace scans, and path-scoped `git diff --check` with expected LF/CRLF warnings only. Focused `cargo test -p zircon_runtime --lib render_velocity_camera_params --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-render-main-chain --message-format short --color never -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture` timed out after 304.7s without compiler/test output; leftover validation Cargo/Rust processes were stopped.

The 2026-06-09 vampire project acceptance slice adds a concrete project-level visibility regression on top of the mesh path. `example_vampire_scene_renders_visible_mesh_pixels` opens `examples/vampire`, imports project assets, loads `res://scenes/main.scene.toml`, disables skybox, renders a 320x240 frame, and requires non-background mesh pixels. The test caught two runtime-facing issues: the project custom material shader had to declare the renderer-owned scene/model/material bind groups consumed by `SceneRenderer`, and the hand-authored primitive placeholder materials need `double_sided = true` until the generated winding is normalized. The same slice keeps the vampire camera angled down into the arena with mesh entities extracted so the screenshot path exercises a real third-person scene rather than fallback UI color.

The 2026-06-09 object velocity pass fix keeps mesh rendering compatible when the product graph includes raw object velocity but the pass is executed outside the forward shadow-map graph. `temporal/velocity/execute_velocity_object.rs` now binds the same fallback forward shadow-receiver group used by ordinary mesh passes before issuing material draws, so shaders that share the current mesh bind layout do not hit missing group 4 validation. `object_velocity_binds_forward_shadow_receiver_group` covers the bind call directly.

The 2026-06-07 mesh vertex attribute follow-up carries M6 mesh tangent and vertex color through the submit-side WGPU path. `MeshVertex` and `GpuMeshVertex` now include tangent/color with neutral defaults for older assets; the glTF importer reads primitive `TANGENT` / `COLOR_0`; `MeshAsset` conversion preserves authored tangent/color attributes and applies glTF-style tangent.xyz plus vertex-color morph deltas in the CPU morph source; the GPU vertex layout appends `@location(5)` tangent and `@location(6)` color after the existing skinning locations; CPU skinning rotates tangent.xyz while preserving handedness and color; and fallback/deferred mesh shaders multiply vertex color into albedo. The follow-up normal-map slice upgrades mesh material texture binding from one albedo texture to a mesh-only material texture set with base-color and normal slots, adds a neutral `NormalMap` fallback resource, and uses `ModelUniform.motion_params.w` to gate tangent-space normal sampling. Forward fallback lighting samples the material normal map through the skinned tangent frame, while the normal prepass writes the same tangent-space result into the scene normal texture consumed by deferred lighting, SSAO, and SSR. Conventional distance-based mesh LOD selections now carry flat `RenderMeshSnapshot.mesh_lod` metadata through draw prep, and queue stats publish the selected draw total as `render.mesh.queue.lod_draw_count`; this is reporting for authored `MeshRenderer.lods`, not Virtual Geometry cluster LOD. The 2026-06-08 mesh sort-field follow-up adds serialized `SceneMeshInstanceAsset.render_queue`, `material_queue`, `order_in_layer`, and `depth_bias`, mirrors those fields on runtime `MeshRenderer`, exposes them through property/reflection access, and propagates them into `GeometryPhaseInput` so same-phase mesh queues can honor the neutral render queue/material queue/order/depth sort contract. Material-owned `render_queue`, `material_queue`, and `depth_bias` now project through `StandardMaterialDescriptor` and `MaterialRuntime`, stay out of `PipelineKey`, and are added to those extracted mesh values during draw construction after resource streaming has prepared materials. Full GPU morph deformation remains later renderer work. Custom material shader authored-layout ABI diagnostics now cover renderer groups 1/2/3, but full texture-array policy remains later renderer work; the vampire acceptance shader is a narrow project-local example that already matches the current renderer bind ABI.

The 2026-06-05 forward mesh shadow-receiver follow-up brings the graph-owned directional shadow map into Forward+ mesh shading without making `shadow-map` mandatory for mesh-only or custom-external pipelines. `opaque-mesh`, `alpha-mask-mesh`, `transparent-mesh`, and deferred transparent mesh descriptors declare a `shadow-map` read, while `RenderPassExecutionContext::reads_transient_texture(...)` lets the mesh executor request the graph shadow map only when the compiled resource is a real transient texture. `MeshPipelineCache` owns mesh bind group 4: a shadow receiver layout, a writable receiver uniform, a permanently disabled fallback uniform, a comparison sampler, and a fallback depth texture view. Graph execution updates the receiver uniform from `ShadowMapRenderer::scene_uniform_for_frame(...)` and binds the graph shadow map through `BaseScenePass`; no-graph and external-read paths bind the fallback view with the disabled uniform so they cannot inherit a previous shadowed frame. The fallback mesh shader, runtime built-in PBR WGSL, and editor default PBR template now match the current `SceneUniform` ABI, pass world position to the fragment stage, sample the shadow map through a 3x3 `textureSampleCompare` PCF term, and apply shadow visibility only to direct light while ambient remains unshadowed. This closes the first Forward+ directional receiver path; cascades, point/spot shadows, custom shader reflection/validation, and a fuller PBR lighting model remain follow-up work. Validation passed scoped `rustfmt --edition 2021` / `--check`, source-contract scans for receiver fallback state and shader/graph contracts, conflict-marker and trailing-whitespace scans, and path-scoped `git diff --check` with expected LF/CRLF warnings only. Clean Cargo validation is not claimed because active workspace/runtime Cargo lanes were still compiling.

The 2026-06-05 material shadow-receiver toggle follow-up adds the first authoring-level receive-side control. `receive_shadows` is a material-owned `.zmaterial` override that defaults to true, is removed when explicitly set back to true during canonical serialization, and is not exposed as a shader property override. Non-boolean values stay in the override map and produce a `PropertyOverrideTypeMismatch` diagnostic at `overrides.receive_shadows` while runtime preparation keeps the default receiver policy. `StandardMaterialDescriptor` and `MaterialRuntime` carry the bool as material state rather than adding it to `PipelineKey`, so materials that differ only by receive-shadow policy share the same mesh pipeline cache entry. Mesh draw construction copies the flag into `ModelUniform.shadow_params.z`; the fallback mesh shader, runtime built-in PBR WGSL, and editor default PBR template early-return full visibility when that lane is disabled before sampling the graph shadow map. This mirrors Bevy's receiver-flag pattern (`MESH_FLAGS_SHADOW_RECEIVER_BIT` in `mesh_types.wgsl` gating shadow fetches in `pbr_functions.wgsl`) while preserving Zircon's current per-draw uniform ABI. Validation passed scoped `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check`, material/shader source-contract scans, stale helper/old-parameter/doc wording scans, conflict-marker and trailing-whitespace scans, and path-scoped `git diff --check` with expected LF/CRLF warnings only; focused Cargo validation was deferred because unrelated `zircon_runtime` Cargo/Rust lanes were already compiling.

The 2026-06-05 material shadow-caster toggle follow-up adds the cast-side counterpart without changing visible mesh queues or pipeline cache keys. `cast_shadows` is a material-owned `.zmaterial` override with the same default-true, canonical true-removal, shader-property filtering, and non-bool `PropertyOverrideTypeMismatch` behavior as `receive_shadows`. `StandardMaterialDescriptor` and `MaterialRuntime` carry it beside `receive_shadows`; mesh draw construction stores it on `MeshDraw`, and `MeshDraw::casts_shadow()` combines material policy with the existing opaque/alpha-mask phase rule. The compiled-scene shadow stage now consumes `MeshDrawPartitions.shadow_casters` instead of every non-transparent draw, while depth prepass, opaque/alpha-mask/transparent rendering, batching eligibility, and `PipelineKey` remain unchanged. This is cast-side filtering only: it does not add cascades, point/spot shadows, per-light layer masks, custom shader reflection, or fuller PBR lighting. Validation passed scoped `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check`, material/shadow-caster source-contract scans, stale shadow-caster/doc wording scans, conflict-marker and trailing-whitespace scans, and path-scoped `git diff --check` with expected LF/CRLF warnings only; focused Cargo validation was deferred because unrelated `zircon_runtime` Cargo/Rust lanes were already compiling.

The 2026-06-06 camera render-layer light extract follow-up aligns extracted lighting with the same camera culling boundary already used by meshes and sprites. `World::build_prepared_viewport_render_packet(...)` and direct `RenderFrameExtract` production now pass the effective camera `RenderLayerSet` into ambient, directional, point, rect, and spot light collectors; each collector filters the owning scene entity through the existing `render_layer_mask` / default legacy mask before projecting render-facing light snapshots. Ambient snapshots still have no `node_id`, so filtering happens before projection from the owning entity. This keeps `LightingExtract`, readiness stats, and the shadow map's first directional-light selection camera-visible only. The boundary is camera culling only: it does not add per-light object masks, cascades, point/spot shadows, or new light-layer authoring APIs. Validation passed scoped `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check`, source scans for camera-layered light collectors and no stale no-argument collector calls, render-extract test coverage for all light families plus explicit camera override, conflict-marker and trailing-whitespace scans, and path-scoped `git diff --check` with expected LF/CRLF warnings only; focused Cargo validation was deferred because unrelated workspace/runtime Cargo and Rust compiler lanes were still active.

The 2026-06-24 Runtime 15 M4 scene world render light collection owner split records `Runtime 15 M4 scene world render light collection owner split` as `runtime_15_scene_world_render_lights_owner_split_static_passed_cargo_deferred`. `scene/world/render.rs` keeps render-frame extraction, camera descriptor projection, visibility handoff, mesh/sprite/post-process/particle wiring, and the shared camera-layer intersection helper, while `scene/world/render/lights.rs` owns ambient, directional, point, rect, and spot light snapshot collection with the existing `RenderLayerSet` filtering and default render-layer fallback. Guard `runtime_15_scene_world_render_light_collectors_are_child_owner` locks the moved collector owner boundary, line budgets, and Runtime 15/status mirror anchors. This is a production owner split only; it does not change light snapshot semantics, light sort order, shadow selection, renderer degradation reporting, or the WGPU light-buffer ABI.

The 2026-06-24 Runtime 15 M3 WGPU render framework lock poison recovery records `Runtime 15 M3 WGPU render framework lock poison recovery` as `runtime_15_wgpu_render_framework_lock_poison_recovery_static_passed_cargo_deferred`. `WgpuRenderFramework::lock_operation()` and `lock_state()` now recover poisoned mutexes, so viewport lifecycle, pipeline mutation, submit, capture, surface, stats, and graphics-debugger access paths keep using the centralized operation/state lock owners without direct `.lock().unwrap()` production panics. `wgpu_render_framework_accessors_recover_poisoned_locks` and `runtime_15_wgpu_render_framework_lock_poison_recovery_guard_covers_wgpu_framework` lock the helper contract and the Runtime 15/status/documentation anchors. This does not change the render framework trait, submit ordering, renderer state machine, product ownership, or graphics-debugger capture semantics.

The 2026-06-25 Runtime 15 M2 graphics render-framework receiver naming hard cutover records `Runtime 15 M2 graphics render-framework receiver naming hard cutover` as `runtime_15_graphics_render_framework_receiver_naming_hard_cutover_static_passed_cargo_deferred`. The render framework owner now names its local `WgpuRenderFramework` receiver/context as `framework: &WgpuRenderFramework` across viewport lifecycle, pipeline mutation, stats/debug query, capture, frame-submission context, camera-loop submit, direct runtime-frame submit, and preflight failure helpers. `runtime_15_render_framework_receiver_uses_framework_name` locks that `graphics/runtime/render_framework` has no non-network `server` token, and both `runtime_non_network_server_naming_is_classified_by_owner` and `non_network_server_naming.py` no longer retain the retired graphics render-framework debt bucket. This is a naming-only hard cutover; it does not change WGPU framework API, lock ownership, submit behavior, capture semantics, or product telemetry.

The 2026-06-23 Plan 09 light-layer typed-mask follow-up moves directional, point, spot, and rect render light snapshots to `RenderLayerSet`. The scene boundary still reads the authoring-side legacy entity mask, but the raw `u32` is wrapped before it reaches `LightingExtract`; light buffer packing then converts back with `to_legacy_mask_lossy()` only when writing `GpuLightData.shadow_slot_layer[1]`. This keeps CPU render DTOs aligned with camera and volume layer contracts while preserving the current WGPU shader-facing 32-bit light buffer ABI. Validation passed scoped rustfmt/check, old direct light-mask assignment scans, diff-level debt scans, line counts, and diff hygiene; focused `pack_light_slices_encodes_directional_shadow_and_layer_contract` Cargo validation timed out after 188 seconds without output or a test binary, and residual cargo/rustc processes were stopped.

The 2026-06-15 Plan 07 PP-M2-S3 hard cut updates the matching scene-authored source for submit-resolved postprocess. `PostProcessSettingsComponent` lives in fixed runtime scene component storage and seeds base bloom, color-grading, and effect-stack settings only from the selected scene camera; explicit request camera snapshots remain external and use defaults. `PostProcessVolumeComponent` forwards active camera-layer-matching global/box/sphere planned DTOs into `PostProcessExtract.volumes`, and submit resolves the base settings plus those DTOs through `resolved_settings_for_camera(...)` before building the product postprocess graph. This is runtime authoring only: project/asset serialization, editor UI, trigger lifecycle, and capsule/convex volume projection stay outside this slice. Focused Cargo validation passed for `render_post_process_extract`, `render_volume_evaluator`, the two scene extract volume filters, and `render_framework_stats_report_volume_effect_stack_product_node_when_authored`.

The 2026-06-15 Plan 07 PP-M3-S1a exposure contract follow-up extends that same submit boundary to exposure. The shared context builder resolves `RenderExposureSettings` together with bloom/color-grading/effect-stack settings, stores it in `FrameSubmissionContext`, and both runtime-frame construction paths project the effective exposure back into `frame.extract.post_process` before renderer execution. Scene extraction seeds this value from camera `exposure_ev100` as manual exposure, while `post.exposure` volume overrides can switch it to histogram mode and blend compensation, EV100 range, percentile, and speed parameters. This does not yet add the WGPU histogram/resolve executors or persistent exposure buffers; it only prevents the future GPU path from needing a second submit-side state channel.

The 2026-06-15 Plan 07 PP-M3-S1b follow-up connects that exposure contract to WGPU. The compiled post-process graph now declares `postprocess.exposure.histogram`, `history.previous.exposure`, and `history.current.exposure`; histogram mode records `post.exposure.histogram` and `post.exposure.resolve`, while manual mode skips the histogram pass and still writes the same current exposure buffer through resolve. `SceneFrameHistoryTextures` owns previous/current exposure buffers, invalidates them with the rest of compatible frame history, and flips them after successful graph execution. Final post-process composition binds the resolved exposure buffer at binding 28 and multiplies it with authored color-grading exposure, keeping manual and histogram modes on one shader path.

The 2026-06-06 camera scheduling diagnostics follow-up keeps `RenderViewExtract.scene_camera_order_report` as the single scheduling evidence source at submit. The shared context builder copies that report into `FrameSubmissionContext`, `update_base_stats(...)` projects active scheduled-camera count and same-order ambiguity-group count into `RenderStats.last_scene_camera_scheduled_count` and `RenderStats.last_scene_camera_order_ambiguity_count`, and core diagnostics records `render.camera.scheduled_count` plus `render.camera.order_ambiguity_count`. Since the 2026-06-18 Plan 09 M1-S2 loop scaffold, generated offscreen submit consumes the descriptor list by running Base and Overlay descriptors as selected-camera child extracts, with shared UI routed only to the terminal child of the selected Base stack. The renderer now also derives per-child scene color/depth first-clear load ops from `RenderCameraClear` and Overlay `clear_depth`, keeps live `scene-color`/`scene-depth` bound to the fixed `OffscreenTarget` views for child-to-child physical reuse, suppresses texture output direct-import/writeback/capture for non-stack-terminal child frames, gates shared viewport record/present ownership to the viewport-terminal child, routes shared `RenderStats`, graphics-debugger capture, and last virtual-geometry debug snapshot writes through `ViewportCameraStackOutputPolicy::owns_shared_viewport_products()`, snapshots `SceneRenderer::last_light_grid_report()` through `SharedViewportProductReports` before `update_stats(...)`, applies selected-camera `viewport_rect` as WGPU viewport/scissor state for graph-raster passes and terminal post-process output-transfer/FXAA/SMAA resolve passes, and includes the selected camera target fingerprint, Base/Overlay render type, and viewport-rect presence in the compiled graph cache key so child submits do not reuse graphs compiled for a different selected-camera shape. Texture child target fingerprints now include the texture `ResourceId`, resolved extent, and `rgba8unorm_srgb` versus `rgba8unorm` format class supplied by target preflight. Temporal color history, the visibility static index, previous motion-vector camera, particle previous sprites, Hybrid GI runtime, Virtual Geometry runtime state, light-grid reports, and virtual-geometry debug snapshots now live in per-selected-camera viewport-record slots, so non-terminal children can keep state and product-debug records without taking over shared viewport stats or capture. Post-process keeps graph-owned intermediates in local viewport coordinates while translating fixed full-frame source reads through the selected camera origin, and Hybrid GI history writeback copies graph-owned `postprocess.global-illumination` into the selected camera's physical history region. Native surface present and direct runtime-frame submit now use the same selected-camera loop and viewport-terminal ownership gate. This is still not final multi-camera target routing: final custom-target composite, remaining custom-target product runs, unified sort-key RenderDoc coverage, and wider RenderDoc/product evidence remain renderer milestones.

The 2026-06-20 PrimarySurface render-order product guard historically added `primary_surface_base_camera_render_order_swap_changes_composite`. It submitted two full-viewport Base cameras to the primary surface, first with green after red and then red after green, and sampled the final capture to prove the later `render_order` Base owns the final composite color. The focused Cargo wrapper generated the lib-test binary but failed while writing the Windows target fingerprint path; direct execution of the generated binary passed the exact test. On 2026-06-23 the current source guard moved to the folder-backed camera-target owner under the Plan 09 product name `render_product_camera_render_order_swap_changes_composite`, and the old `m4_behavior_layers.rs` source test/helper was removed. This closes the PrimarySurface full-viewport Base ordering guard source ownership only; broader custom-target composite/product parity remains a Plan 09 CO-M2 acceptance item, and the migrated guard still needs a fresh focused WGPU rerun.

The PrimarySurface Overlay depth follow-up historically added `primary_surface_overlay_clear_depth_controls_depth_reuse`. It submits a Base camera with a near red mesh that writes depth, then an Overlay camera with a farther green mesh in the same Base stack. With `clear_depth=false`, the center pixels remain red because the Overlay loads Base depth; with `clear_depth=true`, the Overlay clears depth and replaces the center pixels with green. The focused Cargo wrapper passed 1 test in `target\codex-runtime-camera-products-0620`, covering the Plan 09 `render_product_overlay_stack_composites_over_base` clearDepth pair without relying on RenderDoc. On 2026-06-23 the current source guard moved to the folder-backed camera-target owner under the product name `render_product_overlay_stack_composites_over_base`, and the old `m4_behavior_layers.rs` source test/helper path was removed. Scoped rustfmt/static/diff checks passed, but the focused locked rerun was rejected before compilation because the current `Cargo.lock` would need an update under `--locked`; the migrated guard still needs a fresh focused WGPU rerun with the rest of the camera-target group after the lock drift is reconciled.

The adjacent custom-target coexistence guard adds `texture_target_stack_preserves_composite_when_primary_surface_renders_later`. It renders an sRGB texture target stack first, with Base red clear and a clear-none Overlay green mesh, then renders a later PrimarySurface Base blue clear in the same frame. The test reads the prepared texture target and captures the viewport: the texture keeps the red/green stack composite, while the viewport belongs to the later blue PrimarySurface camera. The focused Cargo wrapper timed out during Windows lib-test compile/link, but the background compile produced an updated lib-test binary and direct execution passed the exact test. On 2026-06-23 the current source guard moved out of `m4_behavior_layers.rs` and into `render_product_camera_targets/texture_target.rs` together with the layered overlay and linear-conversion texture-target guards. Broader custom-target composite rules and RenderDoc evidence remain separate acceptance work.

The material-sampling follow-up historically added `texture_target_render_order_feeds_later_primary_surface_material_sample`. It first wrote an sRGB `RenderTarget + Sampled` texture through a texture camera, then rendered a later PrimarySurface Base camera whose mesh material sampled that same texture through `base_color_texture`. The red run failed before the production change with `red=0, blue=17436, total=19200`, proving the old path sampled the ordinary asset texture instead of the prepared render target. After `OutputTargetTextureResource` retained a sampler and `MaterialTextureSet` accepted output-target bindings, the focused Cargo wrapper passed 1 test, and direct binary execution revalidated the PrimarySurface order guard, custom-target coexistence guard, and material-sampling guard.

The 2026-06-23 structure follow-up migrates that RT-to-main material-sampling guard into the folder-backed camera-target owner under the Plan 09 product name `render_product_dual_camera_rt_then_main`. The old `m4_behavior_layers.rs` test was removed, and the canonical guard now uses `RenderFixture::configured_framework(...)` with the other camera-target product cases. This is a test-ownership and acceptance-name migration only; no new production render path was changed. Scoped rustfmt/check, conflict-marker scan, current-test-name scan, line-count scan, and scoped `git diff --check` passed with only line-ending warnings; no new WGPU/Cargo pass is claimed for the migrated guard until the focused `render_product_camera_targets` run is rerun.

The 2026-06-23 wider custom-target guard now lives in `render_product_camera_targets/custom_target/material_sampling.rs` so the multi-RT Plan 09 product scenario does not keep expanding `m4_behavior_layers.rs` or the camera-target root. `custom_target_stacks_feed_later_primary_surface_materials_independently` writes two sRGB Base+Overlay texture targets in one frame, then renders a later PrimarySurface with two sampled-material meshes. The assertions check both prepared targets for independent red/green and blue/red composites and check the final PrimarySurface regions sample their matching targets without cross-target color leakage. This is a test-only acceptance extension; it did not change the production submit, streamer, material binding, or scene-renderer paths. Scoped rustfmt/check passed, and the focused locked Cargo run is blocked by the current workspace `Cargo.lock` / manifest drift before compilation.

The follow-up `custom_target_viewport_regions_feed_later_primary_surface_sample` keeps the scenario in the custom-target viewport owner and exercises one sRGB custom target with two Base camera descriptors that write left/right `RenderViewportRect` regions before a later PrimarySurface samples the prepared texture. The chained follow-up `custom_target_chain_feeds_later_texture_and_primary_surface_samples` adds the remaining same-frame dependency shape inside `custom_target/material_sampling.rs`. It writes a red source sRGB texture target, renders an intermediate texture target whose material samples the source target, then renders a later PrimarySurface whose material samples the intermediate target. The source/intermediate prepared-target center pixels and final PrimarySurface center region all assert the chain resolves through prepared output targets instead of falling back to clears or ordinary asset texture data. This is test-only; scoped rustfmt/static/conflict/whitespace scans passed, but focused Cargo and RenderDoc evidence are still pending.

The late-producer follow-up `custom_target_late_producer_feeds_previous_frame_not_future_sample` now lives in `custom_target/ordering.rs` and covers the explicit Plan 09 ordering rule for consumers that run before a render-target producer. The first frame seeds an sRGB texture target green. The next frame renders an earlier PrimarySurface material that samples that target, then a later texture camera writes the same target red. The assertions require the final target to become red while the PrimarySurface still samples the previous-frame green content, proving the graph does not expose same-frame future camera output to an earlier consumer. This is test-only; scoped rustfmt/static scans passed, while focused Cargo and RenderDoc evidence remain pending.

The overlay-viewport follow-up `custom_target_overlay_inherits_base_viewport_region_before_primary_sample` lives in `custom_target/viewport.rs` and covers the custom-target cross product of Base viewport rect and Overlay inheritance. The composite follow-up `custom_target_two_viewport_stacks_preserve_independent_composites_before_primary_sample` lives in `custom_target/composite.rs` and covers the wider custom-target product rule where two Base+Overlay stacks target different viewport regions of the same sRGB texture before a later PrimarySurface samples it. The assertions read both the prepared custom target and the later PrimarySurface capture, requiring each side's base and overlay colors to remain visible while rejecting cross-viewport leakage.
